## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes standards for the accurate, timely, and compliant submission of Medicare claims for home health services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Proper billing practices are a fundamental requirement of Medicare participation and a core component of the agency's corporate compliance program. Every claim submitted to Medicare must reflect only services that were actually rendered, are clinically supported by complete contemporaneous documentation, were physician-ordered, and meet all Medicare coverage requirements under 42 CFR Part 484 and the Patient-Driven Groupings Model (PDGM). Inaccurate or fraudulent billing exposes the agency to False Claims Act liability, Civil Monetary Penalties, Medicare exclusion, and criminal prosecution. This policy ensures the agency satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR Part 424 (Conditions for Medicare Payment), the CMS Medicare Claims Processing Manual, and the OIG Compliance Program Guidance for Home Health Agencies.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
All certified coders performing diagnosis coding or PDGM classification
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (for clinical documentation completeness and clinical billing correlation)
Compliance Officer (for audit, monitoring, and investigation functions)
Administrator
QAPI Coordinator (for billing quality metrics integration)
Any contracted billing service, clearinghouse, or third-party entity performing billing functions on behalf of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
This policy does not apply to:
Direct patient care delivery staff except to the extent that their documentation directly supports the billing process. Clinical documentation requirements supporting billing are governed by CL-CD-001 through CL-CD-004, CL-OA-001 through CL-OA-019, and CL-CA-001 through CL-CA-007.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall submit Medicare claims only for services that: (a) were actually and completely rendered by a qualified clinician; (b) are supported by complete, contemporaneous, authenticated clinical documentation in the medical record; (c) were ordered by an eligible physician or allowed practitioner through a signed Plan of Care; (d) meet all Medicare coverage criteria including homebound status, skilled care need, and medical necessity; and (e) are coded accurately per current ICD-10-CM guidelines and PDGM classification rules.
4.2 No claim shall be submitted for any service where the required physician certification, face-to-face encounter documentation, OASIS transmission acceptance, or clinical visit documentation is incomplete, expired, or not on file at the time of claim submission.
4.3 All claims shall be submitted within the CMS timely filing deadline (currently 12 months from the date of service) and shall comply with all HIPAA electronic transaction standards (837I institutional claim format).
4.4 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a claims submission calendar, tracking log, and pre-billing verification system for all open episodes to prevent missed filing deadlines and ensure complete documentation support before any claim is released.
4.5 Any claim identified as potentially inaccurate, unsupported by documentation, or otherwise questionable shall be placed on billing hold immediately. Held claims shall not be submitted until the deficiency is resolved, documented, and cleared by the Revenue Cycle Director.
4.6 The agency shall never upcode, unbundle, duplicate bill, bill for services not rendered, or manipulate coding to maximize reimbursement beyond what documentation supports. Any such conduct constitutes grounds for immediate termination and referral to the Compliance Officer for investigation under policies CO-FA-003 and CO-CP-007.
4.7 All billing personnel shall complete initial and annual training on Medicare billing requirements, PDGM payment methodology, False Claims Act obligations, and agency billing policies, with documented competency validation, per policy CO-CP-008.
4.8 Claims submission processes shall be integrated with the agency's corporate compliance program (CO-CP-001), documentation audit program (CO-DC-002), and triangulation audit process (CO-DC-004) to ensure continuous alignment between clinical care delivery, clinical documentation, and billed claims.
4.9 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must not be used for any operational, billing, or compliance purpose. Any revision requires re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| PDGM | Patient-Driven Groupings Model — the CMS payment methodology effective January 1, 2020, that classifies home health episodes into 30-day payment periods based on five factors: admission source and timing, clinical grouping (primary diagnosis), functional impairment level, comorbidity adjustment, and episode timing (early vs. late). |
| 30-Day Payment Period | The unit of payment under PDGM. Each 60-day certification period is divided into two 30-day payment periods, each classified and reimbursed independently. |
| NOA | Notice of Admission — the electronic notification submitted to the MAC within 5 calendar days of the start of care date to initiate the payment episode. Replaced the Request for Anticipated Payment (RAP) effective January 1, 2022. |
| Final Claim | The UB-04 / 837I claim submitted at the end of a 30-day payment period representing the agency's request for the PDGM episode payment. |
| UB-04 / 837I | UB-04 is the standard institutional claim form; 837I is its electronic equivalent used for Medicare home health billing per HIPAA electronic transaction standards. |
| Clean Claim | A claim that passes all pre-submission edits and contains all required data elements for processing by the MAC without the need for additional development or information. |
| Timely Filing | CMS requirement that Medicare claims be submitted no later than 12 months (one calendar year) from the date of service. Claims submitted beyond this deadline are automatically denied and cannot be appealed. |
| OASIS Transmission | Required electronic submission of OASIS assessment data to CMS. OASIS must be transmitted and accepted by CMS before or simultaneously with the final claim for the corresponding 30-day period. |
| MAC | Medicare Administrative Contractor — the regional contractor designated by CMS to process Medicare home health claims for the agency's jurisdiction. |
| Remittance Advice (RA/MRA) | The electronic (835 transaction) or paper explanation of payment, adjustment, or denial issued by the MAC following claim adjudication. Also called Medicare Remittance Advice (MRA). |
| Upcoding | The fraudulent or improper practice of assigning a diagnosis code, PDGM classification, or service code that results in higher reimbursement than the clinical documentation supports. |
| Unbundling | The fraudulent or improper practice of billing separately for services that should be billed together under a bundled payment methodology (e.g., PDGM). |
| Billing Hold | A status applied to a claim preventing its submission until identified deficiencies are investigated and resolved. |
| Pre-Billing Verification | A structured quality check completed before any claim is released for submission, verifying completeness of all required clinical, administrative, and compliance elements. |
| ADR | Additional Documentation Request — a request from the MAC, RAC, or other review entity for the clinical records supporting a submitted or paid claim. Failure to respond to an ADR within the specified timeframe results in automatic denial. |
| False Claims Act | Federal statute (31 U.S.C. §§ 3729–3733) imposing liability on persons and entities that submit false or fraudulent claims to the federal government. Penalties include treble damages plus per-claim penalties currently ranging from $13,946 to $27,894 per false claim (adjusted annually for inflation). |
| 60-Day Rule | ACA requirement (42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d)) mandating that identified overpayments be reported and returned within 60 days of identification. Failure to comply creates a reverse false claim. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Pre-Billing Verification Process
6.1.1 Prior to submitting any final claim (Request for Final Payment) for any 30-day payment period, the Billing Coordinator shall complete the Pre-Billing Verification Checklist (Appendix A) and obtain Revenue Cycle Director sign-off.
6.1.2 The pre-billing verification shall confirm all of the following:

| Step | Verification Item | Required Standard | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.2.1 | Physician certification on file | Signed Plan of Care (485) with valid certification period covering the billed 30-day period. Signature must be original or authenticated electronic; stamped signatures are not accepted. | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.2 | Physician certification signed within required timeframe | Plan of Care signed and dated by physician prior to final claim submission per CMS certification requirements | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.3 | Face-to-face encounter documented | F2F encounter documentation on file and within validity period per policies CL-CA-006 and CL-CA-007 | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.4 | OASIS transmission confirmed | CMS acceptance acknowledgment (validation report) on file confirming successful OASIS data submission for this assessment period | Billing/Clinical Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.5 | All ordered visits delivered or variance documented | Visit frequency delivered matches physician-ordered frequency per CL-SD-025; any variance has documented clinical justification and physician awareness | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.6 | All visit notes completed and locked | Every visit for the 30-day period is documented, authenticated, and locked in the EHR per CL-CD-001 and CL-CD-004 | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.7 | Clinical documentation supports all billed services | Each service type billed (SN, PT, OT, ST, MSW, HHA) has corresponding clinical documentation | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.8 | Homebound status documented | Homebound status narrative present in each visit note and supported in the OASIS per CL-CA-005 | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.9 | ICD-10 codes validated | All diagnosis codes verified against clinical documentation by qualified coding personnel per FN-CM-002 | Certified Coder | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.10 | PDGM classification verified | Clinical group, functional level, comorbidity adjustment, timing, and admission source verified per FN-CM-001 | Certified Coder | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.11 | LUPA threshold status verified | If visit count falls below LUPA threshold, appropriate clinical review completed per FN-CM-005 | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.12 | No active compliance hold on episode | Compliance Officer sign-off obtained if this episode has been flagged by any compliance review, audit, or investigation | Compliance Officer | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.13 | No active ADR for this patient | Confirm no pending ADR that would affect this claim | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.14 | Patient Medicare eligibility confirmed | Patient Medicare eligibility verified for the dates of service | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.1.2.15 | No duplicate billing | Claim checked against submission log to prevent duplicate submission | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |

6.1.3 If any verification item cannot be confirmed as compliant, the claim shall be placed on billing hold immediately. The Revenue Cycle Director shall notify the Director of Nursing in writing within 24 hours of any clinical documentation deficiency preventing claim submission, and the Compliance Officer within 24 hours of any compliance-related hold.
6.1.4 Billing holds shall be logged in the Billing Hold Log (Appendix B) with: patient name, Medicare number, episode dates, hold date, hold reason, responsible party for resolution, expected resolution date, and actual resolution date.
6.1.5 Documentation of the completed Pre-Billing Verification Checklist shall be maintained in the agency's billing system or compliance file as an audit trail entry for each 30-day payment period, retained for a minimum of 7 years.
### 6.2 Claims Submission Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Confirm that the NOA has been submitted and accepted for this episode per FN-BC-006. If NOA is missing, resolve before final claim submission. | Billing Coordinator | Before claim release |
| 6.2.2 | Transmit the final claim electronically via the agency's designated clearinghouse using the 837I institutional claim transaction format per HIPAA electronic transaction standards (45 CFR Parts 160 and 162). | Billing Coordinator | Within 14 calendar days of the end of each 30-day payment period, but never later than the 12-month timely filing deadline |
| 6.2.3 | Confirm clearinghouse acknowledgment (999 transaction — receipt acknowledgment) and MAC acceptance/rejection (277CA transaction — claim acknowledgment) within 2 business days of submission. | Billing Coordinator | Within 2 business days |
| 6.2.4 | If the claim is rejected by the clearinghouse or MAC, identify the rejection reason code, correct the error, and resubmit the corrected claim within 5 business days of rejection notification. | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 business days |
| 6.2.5 | Log the claim in the Claims Submission Log (Appendix C) documenting: patient name, Medicare number, claim control number, 30-day period dates, submission date, billed amount, clearinghouse acknowledgment date, MAC acceptance date, and current status. | Billing Coordinator | Same day as submission |
| 6.2.6 | Submit any applicable adjustments, replacements, or void claims using the appropriate claim frequency code (7 = replacement, 8 = void). Never submit a new original claim to correct a previously adjudicated claim. | Revenue Cycle Director | As needed |

### 6.3 Post-Submission Monitoring

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Monitor CMS Direct Data Entry (DDE) system or equivalent MAC portal for claim status updates on all submitted claims. | Billing Coordinator | Weekly |
| 6.3.2 | Upon receipt of Medicare Remittance Advice (MRA/835 transaction), review each line item for: (a) correct payment amount per PDGM payment group; (b) any adjustments, partial payments, or offsets; (c) denial reason codes requiring investigation or appeal. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 3 business days of MRA receipt |
| 6.3.3 | Reconcile actual payment received against expected PDGM reimbursement per FN-BC-007. Document any payment discrepancy exceeding $250. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days of MRA receipt |
| 6.3.4 | For any denied claim, initiate the denial management process per FN-BC-002 within 5 business days. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days |
| 6.3.5 | For any identified overpayment, initiate the overpayment investigation and refund process per FN-BC-004 within 24 hours. | Compliance Officer / Revenue Cycle Director | Within 24 hours |
| 6.3.6 | Update the Claims Submission Log with final adjudication status, payment amount, and any follow-up actions. | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 business days of adjudication |

### 6.4 Timely Filing Compliance

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a timely filing calendar identifying the 12-month filing deadline for every open 30-day payment period. | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing — updated weekly |
| 6.4.2 | Claims approaching 90 days from the end of the 30-day period without submission shall be flagged as "approaching deadline" in the submission log. | Revenue Cycle Director | At 90 days |
| 6.4.3 | Claims approaching 180 days without submission shall trigger written escalation to the Administrator and Director of Nursing with mandatory resolution within 14 calendar days. | Revenue Cycle Director | At 180 days |
| 6.4.4 | Claims approaching 300 days without submission shall trigger written escalation to the CFO and Compliance Officer as a potential revenue loss event. | Revenue Cycle Director | At 300 days |
| 6.4.5 | Any claim that expires beyond the 12-month timely filing deadline shall be reported as an unbillable loss, documented in the billing loss log, and root cause analyzed within 14 calendar days. | Revenue Cycle Director / Compliance Officer | Within 14 calendar days of deadline expiration |

### 6.5 Billing Integrity Controls

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Billing staff shall not submit claims for services that were not actually rendered. No exceptions. | All Billing Personnel | Ongoing |
| 6.5.2 | Billing staff shall not alter, backdate, or falsify any billing data, claim submission date, or supporting documentation. | All Billing Personnel | Ongoing |
| 6.5.3 | Billing staff shall not assign or accept diagnosis codes that do not have supporting physician documentation in the medical record. | All Billing/Coding Personnel | Ongoing |
| 6.5.4 | Billing staff who identify a potentially improper claim — whether submitted by themselves or others — shall report the concern to the Compliance Officer immediately using the compliance hotline or direct report per CO-CP-006. Non-retaliation protections per CO-CP-005 apply. | All Billing Personnel | Immediately upon identification |
| 6.5.5 | The Compliance Officer shall investigate any billing integrity concern within 7 calendar days of receipt per CO-CP-007. | Compliance Officer | Within 7 calendar days |

### 6.6 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Clinical documentation deficiency prevents claim submission | Revenue Cycle Director notifies Director of Nursing in writing | DON assigns clinical staff to complete documentation within 7 calendar days. If unresolved, escalate to Administrator. | DON response within 24 hours; documentation completion within 7 calendar days |
| Timely filing deadline within 30 calendar days with unresolved deficiency | Revenue Cycle Director escalates to Administrator and Director of Nursing jointly | Emergency clinical documentation remediation. Administrator may direct prioritization of documentation completion. | Within 24 hours of escalation |
| Claim denial rate exceeds 5% in any 30-day period | Revenue Cycle Director reports to CFO and Compliance Officer in writing | Root cause analysis initiated per FN-BC-002. Corrective action plan within 30 calendar days. | Report within 5 business days; RCA within 30 calendar days |
| Clean claim rate falls below 95% for 2 consecutive months | Revenue Cycle Director escalates to CFO | Root cause analysis of rejection patterns. System, training, or process correction within 30 calendar days. | Report within 5 business days; correction within 30 calendar days |
| Potential False Claims Act exposure identified | Compliance Officer and legal counsel notified | Investigation per CO-CP-007. No further claims submitted for affected episodes pending investigation. Self-disclosure considered per CO-FA-003. | Within 24 hours |
| CMS audit or ADR request received | Compliance Officer and Administrator notified | ADR response protocol per FN-BC-002. Full records compiled and submitted within MAC-specified deadline (typically 30–45 days). | Within 24 hours of receipt; records submitted per deadline |
| Billing staff member suspected of fraudulent billing activity | Compliance Officer notified | Investigation per CO-CP-007. Staff member suspended from billing duties pending investigation. HR Director notified per HR-ER-002. | Within 24 hours |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claims submitted within timely filing deadline | Review of claims submission log and timely filing calendar; zero expired claims | 100% |
| Clean claim rate (accepted on first electronic submission) | Clearinghouse acceptance report; 999/277CA confirmations | ≥ 95% |
| Denial rate | Monthly denial analysis per FN-BC-002 | ≤ 5% |
| Pre-Billing Verification Checklist completed for all 30-day periods | Audit of completed Appendix A checklists against total episodes billed | 100% |
| All billing holds resolved within 14 calendar days | Billing Hold Log review | 100% within 14 calendar days |
| Billing staff annual training completion | HR training records | 100% |
| Policy acknowledgment current for all in-scope personnel | Appendix D file review | 100% within 14 calendar days of effective date |
| No claims submitted for services not rendered | Triangulation audit per CO-DC-004 | Zero findings |
| All identified overpayments refunded within 60 days | Overpayment tracking log per FN-BC-004 | 100% |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors and MAC auditors reviewing the agency's billing practices will specifically verify:
Evidence that the agency has a billing quality control process. Surveyors will request evidence of pre-billing verification procedures. Agencies that cannot demonstrate a structured verification process risk Standard-level deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.100 (compliance with federal laws).
Evidence that billed services match clinical documentation. Auditors will compare billed claims to visit notes, Plan of Care orders, and OASIS assessments. Any mismatch between billed services and clinical documentation will be cited.
Evidence that physician certification is complete before billing. Auditors will verify that the signed 485 is on file and dated appropriately for each billed period.
Evidence that face-to-face encounter documentation supports billing. Auditors will review F2F documentation for completeness and validity.
Evidence that coding is supported by the medical record. OIG/RAC auditors will specifically test whether ICD-10 codes billed on claims match physician-documented diagnoses. Unsupported codes will result in overpayment demand.
Evidence that billing staff have received compliance training. Surveyors may request training records for billing personnel.
Evidence that billing concerns are reported and investigated. Compliance program auditors (OIG) will look for evidence of a functioning reporting system and investigation process.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claims submitted before physician certification is signed | Automatic denial; potential overpayment if claim paid before signature obtained | Pre-billing verification step 6.1.2.1; CL-CP-009 physician signature tracking |
| Claims submitted before OASIS is transmitted/accepted | Claim rejection or payment hold | Pre-billing verification step 6.1.2.4 |
| Visit notes incomplete or unauthenticated at time of billing | Claim not supported by documentation; audit vulnerability | Pre-billing verification step 6.1.2.6; CL-CD-004 timely documentation lock |
| Billing for missed or rescheduled visits that were not actually delivered | False claim exposure | Pre-billing verification step 6.1.2.5; CL-SD-024/CL-SD-025 visit compliance |
| ICD-10 codes not supported by physician documentation | Upcoding; False Claims Act exposure; PDGM misclassification | Pre-billing verification step 6.1.2.9; FN-CM-002 coding accuracy policy |
| Timely filing deadline missed due to documentation delays | Permanent revenue loss; no appeal rights | Timely filing calendar §6.4; escalation at 90/180/300 days |
| No pre-billing quality check before claim submission | Multiple avoidable errors; high denial rate | Appendix A mandatory for 100% of claims |
| Billing staff aware of problem but not reporting | False Claims Act whistleblower liability; compliance program failure | §6.5.4 mandatory reporting; CO-CP-005 non-retaliation |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All billing coordinators, coding personnel, and the Revenue Cycle Director shall complete initial training on this policy within 14 calendar days of hire or assignment to billing functions. Initial training shall be conducted by the Revenue Cycle Director or Compliance Officer and must cover: (a) Medicare home health billing requirements and PDGM payment methodology; (b) the pre-billing verification process and use of Appendix A; (c) claims submission procedures; (d) billing hold procedures; (e) timely filing requirements and escalation protocol; (f) prohibited billing practices; (g) False Claims Act liability and penalties; (h) the obligation and process for reporting billing concerns.
10.2 All billing personnel shall complete annual refresher training covering the topics above plus any CMS billing rule changes effective in the current year. Annual training shall include a competency assessment (minimum passing score: 80%).
10.3 All personnel within scope of this policy shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix D) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new hire/assignment.
10.4 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a training tracking log documenting training dates, topics, competency scores, and acknowledgment dates for all billing personnel. Training non-compliance shall be reported to the CFO and HR Director within 7 calendar days of any deadline lapse.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, billing, or compliance purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision requires: (a) review by the CFO and Compliance Officer; (b) approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (c) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (d) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the CFO with notification to the Compliance Officer and the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.
## Appendix A — Pre-Billing Verification Checklist

# FN-BC-002 — Claims Denial Management & Appeals
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-002__ |
| Title | Claims Denial Management & Appeals |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10__ |
| Status | ACTIVE__ |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED__ |
| Review Cycle | Annual__ |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10__ |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR Part 405 Subpart I (Medicare Appeals), 42 CFR § 484.100, CMS IOM Pub. 100-04, CMS IOM Pub. 100-20 (Appeals), False Claims Act, ACA 60-Day Rule |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes a systematic, comprehensive process for tracking, analyzing, categorizing, appealing, and preventing Medicare claims denials at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Unmanaged denials result in revenue loss, cash flow disruption, and undetected systemic billing or documentation deficiencies. Every denial — regardless of amount — must be investigated for root cause because even small denials may indicate patterns of non-compliance that, if uncorrected, constitute systemic billing errors or False Claims Act exposure. This policy ensures that every denial is reviewed within defined timeframes, appropriately appealed when meritorious, written off only when justified and authorized, and analyzed for root cause to drive continuous improvement through the agency's QAPI program.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
All certified coders
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (for clinical denial review)
Compliance Officer (for denial pattern analysis and compliance correlation)
Administrator
QAPI Coordinator (for denial trending integration with quality program)
Any contracted billing service performing denial management functions
This policy does not apply to:
Non-Medicare denials from commercial payers or Medicare Advantage plans are managed using similar principles but may have different appeal timelines and processes. Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain payer-specific appeal protocols for non-Medicare payers.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formal denial management program that tracks 100% of Medicare claim denials from initial receipt through final resolution (paid on appeal, written off with authorization, or all appeal levels exhausted).
4.2 Every denial shall be reviewed within 5 business days of receipt to determine: (a) the denial reason code and category; (b) whether the denial is technically or clinically valid; (c) whether the denial is appealable; (d) the appropriate level and strategy of appeal; (e) whether the denial reflects a systemic documentation, coding, or billing deficiency requiring broader corrective action.
4.3 All clinical denials (medical necessity, homebound status, documentation insufficiency, coverage criteria) shall be reviewed jointly by the Revenue Cycle Director and Director of Nursing before any appeal decision or write-off is authorized. The Director of Nursing's clinical assessment of appeal merit shall be documented.
4.4 The agency shall pursue all meritorious appeals through the full Medicare administrative appeals process. No claim with a legitimate clinical and documentation basis shall be written off without exhausting at least the Redetermination (Level 1) level of appeal.
4.5 No clinical denial shall be written off without the countersignature of the CFO and a written rationale documenting why the denial is valid or why appeal would be non-meritorious. The Write-Off Authorization Form (Appendix C) is required.
4.6 Denial trending data shall be reported to the CFO monthly, included in the quarterly compliance report to the Governing Body per GV-GB-001 §6.2.3, and integrated with QAPI performance monitoring per QA-PI-002.
4.7 Any single denial category accounting for more than 3% of total claims submitted in any 90-day period shall trigger a formal root cause analysis and corrective action plan within 30 calendar days per QA-AE-002 and QA-AE-003.
4.8 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain current knowledge of all Medicare appeal deadlines, appeal level requirements, and amount-in-controversy thresholds, updated annually.
4.9 ADR (Additional Documentation Request) responses shall be treated as the highest priority billing activity. All ADR deadlines shall be tracked on a separate ADR log with escalation protocol.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Denial | A determination by Medicare or its contractors that a claim, or a portion of a claim, will not be paid as submitted. Denials may be full (entire claim denied) or partial (portion of claim denied or adjusted). |
| Initial Determination | The first decision by the MAC on a submitted claim, resulting in either payment or denial. |
| Redetermination (Level 1) | First level of the Medicare administrative appeals process — a de novo review by the MAC that processed the original claim. Must be filed within 120 calendar days of the initial determination. MAC must decide within 60 calendar days. |
| Reconsideration (Level 2) | Second level — review by a Qualified Independent Contractor (QIC). Must be filed within 180 calendar days of the Redetermination decision. QIC must decide within 60 calendar days. |
| ALJ Hearing (Level 3) | Third level — hearing before an Administrative Law Judge at the Office of Medicare Hearings and Appeals (OMHA). Available only when the amount in controversy meets the current CMS threshold (adjusted annually; $190 for 2025). Must be filed within 60 calendar days of QIC decision. |
| Medicare Appeals Council (Level 4) | Fourth level — review by the Medicare Appeals Council within the Departmental Appeals Board. Must be filed within 60 calendar days of ALJ decision. |
| Federal District Court (Level 5) | Fifth and final level — judicial review in federal court. Available only when the amount in controversy meets the current CMS threshold (adjusted annually; $1,900 for 2025). Must be filed within 60 calendar days of Medicare Appeals Council decision. |
| AD__ | Additional Documentation Request — a request from the MAC, RAC, CERT, or ZPIC/UPIC for clinical records supporting a submitted or paid claim. Non-response results in automatic denial. |
| RA__ | Recovery Audit Contractor — CMS contractor tasked with identifying and recovering overpayments from Medicare providers through post-payment review. |
| CERT | Comprehensive Error Rate Testing — CMS program measuring improper payment rates across Medicare claims through statistical sampling. |
| ZPIC/UPIC | Zone Program Integrity Contractor / Unified Program Integrity Contractor — CMS contractor responsible for investigating suspected fraud, waste, and abuse. |
| Appeal Merit Assessment | The joint clinical-billing review determining whether a denial is appealable based on the strength of clinical documentation, coding accuracy, and regulatory compliance. |
| Write-Off | The accounting action of removing an uncollectable denied balance from accounts receivable after all appropriate collection or appeal efforts are exhausted, with required CFO authorization. |
| Demand Bill | A claim submitted at the patient's written request when Medicare is expected to deny coverage, used in conjunction with an ABN per FN-BC-003. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Denial Receipt and Logging

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Upon receipt of any Medicare Remittance Advice (MRA/835 transaction) or denial letter containing a denied or adjusted claim, the Billing Coordinator shall log the denial in the Denial Tracking Log (Appendix A) within 1 business day. Log entries shall include: patient name, Medicare number, claim control number, denial date, denial reason code(s), denial reason description, denial category (per §6.2), dollar amount denied, and 30-day period dates__ | Billing Coordinator | Within 1 business day of receipt |
| 6.1.2 | The Billing Coordinator shall immediately flag any denial involving a dollar amount exceeding $5,000 or any denial reason code associated with fraud/abuse investigation (e.g., CERT/RAC/UPIC-related denial) for immediate Compliance Officer notification. | Billing Coordinator | Same day |
| 6.1.3 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall review all new denials within 3 business days of logging. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 3 business days |

### 6.2 Denial Classification
6.2.1 The Revenue Cycle Director shall classify each denial within 3 business days of logging using the following standardized category system:

| Denial Category | Description | Primary Owner | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Administrative — Eligibility | Patient eligibility or enrollment issue | Billing Coordinator | Medicare number error, coverage gap, MSP issue__ |
| Administrative — Technical | Incorrect data elements, missing fields, duplicate claim | Billing Coordinator | Wrong provider NPI, date of service error, duplicate claim |
| Administrative — Timely Filing | Claim submitted beyond 12-month filing deadline | Revenue Cycle Director | Expired filing deadline |
| Clinical — Medical Necessity | Services determined not medically necessary by MAC/RA__ | Director of Nursing + Revenue Cycle Director | MAC determination that skilled services not required__ |
| Clinical — Homebound | Homebound status documentation insufficient | Director of Nursing + Revenue Cycle Director | Insufficient homebound narrative__ |
| Clinical — Documentation | Clinical documentation insufficient to support services billed | Director of Nursing + Revenue Cycle Director | Missing visit notes, unauthenticated records |
| Clinical — Coverage Criteria | Patient does not meet Medicare home health coverage criteria | Director of Nursing + Revenue Cycle Director | No skilled need, custodial-only care |
| Coding | Incorrect ICD-10, PDGM classification, or OASIS error | Certified Coder + Revenue Cycle Director | Unspecified code, incorrect clinical group, coding not supported by documentation__ |
| Certification — Physician | Missing or expired physician certification, unsigned 485 | Revenue Cycle Director | Unsigned Plan of Care, late signature__ |
| Certification — F2F | Missing or expired face-to-face encounter | Revenue Cycle Director | No F2F on file, F2F expired |
| OASIS — Transmission | OASIS not transmitted or rejected by CMS | Clinical Coordinator | Transmission failure, validation error__ |
| COB — Coordination of Benefits | Payer sequencing error, other insurance primary | Billing Coordinator | MSP, workers comp, auto accident__ |
| LUPA | Visits fell below PDGM threshold; per-visit adjustment | Revenue Cycle Director | Below threshold visits — not a denial per se, but a payment reduction |

### 6.3 Appeal Merit Assessment

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | For administrative denials: Revenue Cycle Director determines whether the error is correctable and resubmittable without appeal. If correctable, correct and resubmit within 5 business days. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days |
| 6.3.2 | For clinical denials: Revenue Cycle Director and Director of Nursing shall jointly review the clinical record, denial reason, and documentation quality to determine appeal merit. The Director of Nursing shall provide a written clinical assessment of whether the documentation supports the billed services. | Revenue Cycle Director + Director of Nursing | Within 5 business days of denial classification__ |
| 6.3.__ | For coding denials: Revenue Cycle Director and Certified Coder shall review the coding against clinical documentation to determine if coding was correct or if a coding error occurred. | Revenue Cycle Director + Coder | Within 5 business days |
| 6.3.4 | Based on the review, the Revenue Cycle Director shall make one of the following decisions (documented in Appendix A): | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days of classification |


| Decision | Criteria | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| APPEAL | Sufficient clinical, coding, and/or documentation basis exists to challenge the denial | Proceed to §6.4 — file appeal at appropriate level |
| CORRECT & RESUBMIT | Denial was due to a correctable administrative or technical error | Correct error and resubmit claim within 5 business days |
| WRITE-OFF | Denial is valid (e.g., untimely filing, confirmed non-covered service, no documentation support) and no appeal basis exists | Complete Write-Off Authorization (Appendix C) — requires CFO countersignature |
| ESCALATE TO COMPLIANCE | Denial indicates potential systemic billing, coding, or documentation issue, or potential FWA concern | Notify Compliance Officer within 24 hours; initiate investigation per CO-CP-007__ |

### 6.4 Medicare Administrative Appeals Process
6.4.1 Level 1 — Redetermination (MAC)

| Element | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Filing Deadline | 120 calendar days from the date of the initial determination |
| Filed With | MAC that processed the original claim |
| Submission Requirements | Written request for Redetermination; copy of MRA/initial determination; complete medical record for the episode; written clinical argument addressing each denial reason code; any additional supporting documentation not previously submitted |
| MAC Decision Deadline | 60 calendar days from receipt of request__ |
| Tracking | Log appeal filing date, deadline for MAC decision, and follow-up dates in Appendix A |


| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1.1 | Prepare Redetermination request package including clinical argument, medical record, and supporting documentation | Revenue Cycle Director (billing component); Director of Nursing (clinical argument) | Within 30 calendar days of initial determination (to allow processing time well before the 120-day deadline) |
| 6.4.1.2 | Submit Redetermination request to MAC via certified mail, fax, or electronic submission per MAC instructions | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 60 calendar days of initial determination (preferred — never later than 120 days) |
| 6.4.1.3 | Confirm receipt by MAC; log confirmation in Appendix A | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 business days of submission__ |
| 6.4.1.4 | Track MAC decision deadline (60 days from receipt); escalate to CFO if no decision received within 55 days | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing__ |
| 6.4.1.5 | Upon receipt of Redetermination decision: if favorable — log outcome and ensure payment processing; if unfavorable — proceed to Level 2 assessment within 10 business days | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days of decision |

6.4.2 Level 2 — Reconsideration (QIC)

| Element | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Filing Deadline | 180 calendar days from the date of the Redetermination decision |
| Filed With | Qualified Independent Contractor (QIC) designated by CMS |
| Submission Requirements | Copy of Redetermination decision; complete medical record; updated written argument addressing Redetermination rationale; any new evidence |
| QIC Decision Deadline | 60 calendar days from receipt |


| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.2.1 | Revenue Cycle Director and Director of Nursing reassess appeal merit in light of Redetermination denial rationale | Revenue Cycle Director + DO__ | Within 10 business days of Redetermination decision |
| 6.4.2.2 | If appeal merit confirmed, prepare QIC Reconsideration request with updated argument | Revenue Cycle Director + DON | Within 60 calendar days of Redetermination decision (preferred) |
| 6.4.2.3 | Submit to QIC; confirm receipt; log in Appendix A | Revenue Cycle Director | Never later than 180 calendar days from Redetermination decision |
| 6.4.2.4 | Track QIC decision and process outcome as per 6.4.1.5 | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing |

6.4.3 Level 3 — ALJ Hearing (OMHA)

| Element | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Filing Deadline | 60 calendar days from the date of the QIC Reconsideration decision |
| Amount in Controversy | Must meet current CMS threshold ($190 for 2025 — adjusted annually) |
| Filed With | Office of Medicare Hearings and Appeals (OMHA) |
| CFO Authorization Required | Yes — Level 3 and above require CFO written authorization |


| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.3.__ | Revenue Cycle Director calculates amount in controversy and confirms eligibility for ALJ hearing | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days of QIC decision |
| 6.4.3.2 | Revenue Cycle Director prepares recommendation for CFO including: denial history, amounts, appeal arguments, probability of success | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days |
| 6.4.3.3 | CFO provides written authorization to proceed or written directive to close the appeal | CFO | Within 5 business days of recommendation |
| 6.4.3.4 | If authorized, submit ALJ hearing request to OMHA | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 60 calendar days of QIC decision |
| 6.4.3.__ | If hearing requires testimony or representation, coordinate with legal counsel | Revenue Cycle Director + Administrator | As scheduled by OMHA |

6.4.4 Levels 4 and 5
Level 4 (Medicare Appeals Council) and Level 5 (Federal District Court) shall be pursued only upon written authorization from both the CFO and Administrator, with legal counsel engagement required. The decision to proceed to Level 4 or 5 shall be reported to the Governing Body.
### 6.5 ADR Response Protocol

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Upon receipt of any ADR (from MAC, RAC, CERT, or ZPIC/UPIC), the Revenue Cycle Director shall immediately log the request in the ADR Tracking Log (Appendix D) and notify the Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer in writing | Revenue Cycle Director | Same day as receipt |
| 6.5.2 | Director of Nursing shall compile the complete medical record for the requested patient/episode within 10 calendar days. Record shall include: all visit notes, Plan of Care, OASIS, physician orders, F2F documentation, and any other clinical documentation | Director of Nursing | Within 10 calendar days of ADR receipt__ |
| 6.5.3 | Revenue Cycle Director shall compile billing records: claim(s), remittance advice, coding worksheets | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 calendar days |
| 6.5.4 | Compliance Officer shall review the complete record package for any compliance concerns before submission | Compliance Officer | Within 5 calendar days of package compilation__ |
| 6.5.__ | Revenue Cycle Director shall submit the complete documentation package to the requesting entity via the specified submission method within the specified deadline (typically 30–45 days from ADR date__ | Revenue Cycle Director | Per ADR deadline — never late__ |
| 6.5.__ | Confirm receipt/submission acknowledgment and log in Appendix D | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 2 business days of submission |
| 6.5.7 | If the ADR deadline is approaching (within 7 calendar days) without completed submission, immediately escalate to the Administrator | Revenue Cycle Director | Immediately |
| 6.5.8 | Never alter, backdate, add to, or fabricate any documentation in response to an ADR. Submit only documentation that existed in the record at the time of the original service. | All personnel | Always__ |

### 6.6 Denial Trending and Root Cause Analysis

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Revenue Cycle Director shall produce a Monthly Denial Analysis Report (Appendix B) by the 15th of the following month. Report shall include: total denials, denial rate, denial by category, appeal filing rate, appeal win rate by level, top 3 denial reasons, dollar impact, and trending comparison to prior 3 months. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly — by 15th of following month |
| 6.6.2 | Monthly Denial Analysis Report shall be reviewed by CFO within 5 business days of receipt | CFO | Within 5 business days |
| 6.6.3 | Quarterly denial summary shall be included in the Compliance Officer's quarterly report to the Governing Body per GV-GB-001 §6.2.3.2 | Compliance Officer | Quarterly |
| 6.6.4 | Denial data shall be integrated with the QAPI dashboard per QA-PI-002 and reported to the QAPI Committee per QA-PG-003 | QAPI Coordinator | Quarterly__ |
| 6.6.__ | Any denial category accounting for ≥ 3% of total claims in any rolling 90-day period shall trigger a formal Root Cause Analysis (RCA) within 30 calendar days per QA-AE-002 | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer + DO__ | Within 30 calendar days of trigger__ |
| 6.6.__ | RCA findings shall result in a documented Corrective Action Plan (CAP) per QA-AE-003 with defined actions, owners, deadlines, and measurable outcomes | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer | Within 14 calendar days of RCA completion__ |
| 6.6.__ | CAP effectiveness shall be measured at 30, 60, and 90 calendar days post-implementation, with results documented and reported to CFO and Compliance Officer | Revenue Cycle Director | 30/60/90 days post-implementation |

### 6.7 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Overall denial rate exceeds 5% for any calendar month | Revenue Cycle Director reports to CFO and Compliance Officer in writing | Immediate denial category analysis; RCA if pattern identified per §6.6.5 | Report within 5 business days |
| Denial rate exceeds 5% for 2 consecutive months | CFO escalates to Administrator and Governing Body | Formal corrective action plan per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.3 | CAP within 30 calendar days |
| ADR response deadline approaching (within 7 days) without completed package | Revenue Cycle Director escalates to Administrator | Emergency record compilation and submission | Immediately__ |
| ADR deadline missed | Compliance Officer notified immediately | Incident reported per RM-ER-002; root cause analysis; corrective action | Within 24 hours |
| Denial pattern suggests systemic upcoding or improper billing | Compliance Officer initiates investigation per CO-CP-007 | Investigation per CO-CP-007; potential FN-BC-004 overpayment process; potential OIG self-disclosure per CO-FA-003 | Within 7 calendar days |
| Single denial exceeding $10,000 | Revenue Cycle Director notifies CFO and Compliance Officer | Priority appeal assessment with DO__ | Within 24 hours |
| Level 3 (ALJ) or higher appeal under consideration | Revenue Cycle Director prepares recommendation for CFO | CFO authorization required; legal counsel consulted | Per §6.4.3 |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Overall denial rate | Total denials ÷ total claims submitted per month | ≤ 5% |
| Denial logged within 1 business day of receipt | Denial Tracking Log timestamp audit | 100% |
| Appeal merit assessment completed within 5 business days | Denial Tracking Log review date | 100% |
| Appeal filed for all meritorious clinical denials | Count of clinical denials with appeal merit assessment "APPEAL" vs. appeals actually filed | 100% |
| Redetermination appeal filed within 60 days of initial determination (preferred) | Appeal filing date audit | 100% within 60 days (mandatory: within 120 days) |
| Appeal win rate — Redetermination (Level 1) | Won appeals ÷ total appeals filed at Level 1 | ≥ 60% |
| ADR responses submitted on time | ADR Tracking Log deadline compliance | 100% |
| Monthly Denial Analysis Report completed | Report file review | 100% — by 15th of following month |
| Write-offs authorized per procedure | Write-Off Authorization Form (Appendix C) on file for all write-offs | 100% |
| RCA triggered for any category ≥ 3% | Denial category analysis vs. RCA records | 100% |

### 8.2 Surveyor and Auditor Expectations
MAC/RAC auditors will request documentation supporting denied and overturned claims. Agencies must demonstrate a functioning denial management process with tracking, appeals, and trending.
OIG compliance auditors expect evidence that denial patterns are analyzed for systemic root causes and that the agency has a corrective action process. Agencies that simply accept denials without investigation risk a finding of inadequate compliance program.
CMS surveyors reviewing 42 CFR § 484.100 (compliance with federal laws) will look for evidence that billing processes include quality control and error correction.
CERT auditors will request complete medical records for sampled claims. Non-response results in automatic improper payment finding.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Denials not tracked systematically | Unknown revenue loss; unidentified billing patterns; unable to demonstrate compliance program functioning | Appendix A mandatory for 100% of denials; §6.1 |
| Clinical denials written off without clinical review | Meritorious appeals missed; revenue loss; potential compliance indicator ignored | §6.3.2 joint DON review required for all clinical denials |
| Clinical denials written off without CFO authorization | Lack of financial oversight; potential unreported overpayment issues | §4.5 and Appendix C required |
| ADR deadline missed | Automatic denial; cannot be appealed; direct revenue loss and compliance finding | §6.5; ADR Tracking Log (Appendix D) with escalation at 7-day warning__ |
| No denial trending or root cause analysis | Systemic billing/documentation problems continue uncorrected | §6.6; monthly report (Appendix B); 3% RCA trigger__ |
| Appeal deadlines missed | Loss of appeal rights; revenue permanently lost | §6.4 appeal timeline tracking in Appendix A |
| Documentation altered or added after ADR is received | Potential federal obstruction charge; False Claims Act exposure | §6.5.8 prohibition — submit only existing documentation |
| Denial data not reported to Governing Body | Governing Body cannot fulfill financial oversight under 42 CFR § 484.105 | §4.6; quarterly reporting per GV-GB-001 §6.2.3/§6.2.5 |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All billing coordinators, coding personnel, and the Revenue Cycle Director shall receive training on the denial management process within 14 calendar days of hire or assignment. Training shall cover: (a) denial logging requirements; (b) denial category classification; (c) appeal merit assessment process; (d) Medicare appeals process (all 5 levels); (e) appeal timeline requirements; (f) ADR response protocol; (g) write-off authorization requirements; (h) denial trending and RCA triggers; (i) False Claims Act implications of billing patterns.
10.2 The Director of Nursing shall receive training on clinical denial review responsibilities and the clinical argument development process.
10.3 Annual refresher training on denial management procedures, updated appeal thresholds and deadlines, and denial trending results.
10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix E) within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 Maintained under EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the current approved version is valid. Superseded versions archived and marked.
11.3 Substantive revisions require CFO and Compliance Officer review, Governing Body approval, and re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions approved by CFO with notification to Compliance Officer and Governing Body.

| 6 | Patient eligibility lapse — not correctable | ☐ |

# FN-BC-003 — Patient Billing & Financial Responsibility
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-003 |
| Title | Patient Billing & Financial Responsibility |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 484.50 (Patient Rights — Financial Information), 42 CFR § 489.20 (Provider Agreements), CMS ABN Requirements (CMS-R-131), Fair Debt Collection Practices Act (15 U.S.C. § 1692), No Surprises Act (where applicable), HIPAA Privacy Rule (45 CFR § 164.502) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes comprehensive standards for patient billing, financial responsibility notification, Advance Beneficiary Notice of Noncoverage (ABN) issuance, billing statement management, and collection of patient financial obligations at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Under 42 CFR § 484.50(a)(3), patients must be informed of expected payment sources and any charges for which the patient may be responsible. Patients have a fundamental right to understand their financial responsibility before and during the receipt of services. The agency has a legal, ethical, and regulatory obligation to bill patients accurately, transparently, and in compliance with all applicable federal, state, and consumer protection laws.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
All intake coordinators (financial screening at admission)
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (ABN clinical determinations)
Compliance Officer
Administrator
Any contracted billing or collection service acting on behalf of the agency
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall provide every patient (or authorized representative) with a clear, written explanation of their financial responsibility — including applicable insurance benefits, cost-sharing obligations, and any anticipated non-covered services — prior to or at the time of admission, before services begin.
4.2 The agency shall inform each patient of the toll-free CMS Home Health Hotline number and the agency's own billing inquiry contact information at admission per 42 CFR § 484.50(a)(3).
4.3 Advance Beneficiary Notices of Noncoverage (ABNs) shall be issued to Medicare fee-for-service beneficiaries using the current CMS-mandated form (CMS-R-131) whenever the agency has reason to believe a specific item or service will not be covered by Medicare. ABNs shall be issued before the non-covered service is provided and shall never be used as routine pre-admission documents or blanket waivers.
4.4 Patient billing statements shall be accurate, itemized, clearly readable, and include the agency's billing inquiry contact information. Statements shall be issued monthly for all open patient balances.
4.5 The agency shall not discriminate in billing practices based on race, color, national origin, disability, age, sex, religion, or any other protected characteristic under federal or state law.
4.6 Patient billing complaints and disputes shall be received, investigated, and resolved within 14 calendar days of receipt, documented per policy OP-PA-001, and shall not result in any interruption of clinically necessary services during the dispute period.
4.7 Collection activities shall be conducted in compliance with the Fair Debt Collection Practices Act (FDCPA) and applicable California state debt collection laws. No collection activity shall be initiated while a valid billing dispute is pending or a charity care application is under review.
4.8 No patient shall be denied medically necessary services due to inability to pay without first being screened for charity care or financial assistance eligibility per policy FN-FP-004.
4.9 All financial responsibility documentation shall be maintained in the patient's billing record and retained per CO-HP-007.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| ABN | Advance Beneficiary Notice of Noncoverage — CMS-mandated written notice (Form CMS-R-131) given to Medicare fee-for-service beneficiaries before providing items or services that Medicare is likely to deny. The ABN transfers financial responsibility to the patient only if properly issued before the service. |
| Financial Responsibility | The portion of charges for which a patient (or their secondary payer) is responsible after primary payer benefits are applied. |
| Cost-Sharing | Medicare deductibles, coinsurance, and copayments applicable to the patient. Under the Medicare home health benefit, covered skilled services have no patient cost-sharing (no copay, no deductible). However, some items (e.g., DME ordered through the agency, non-covered services) may have patient financial responsibility. |
| NOMNC | Notice of Medicare Non-Coverage — CMS-mandated notice (CMS Form CMS-10123-NOMNC) informing a Medicare beneficiary that home health services are ending. Must be delivered at least 2 calendar days before the last covered visit. |
| Itemized Statement | A billing statement listing each specific service rendered, the date of service, the charge for each service, and any payments or adjustments applied — as opposed to a summary or lump-sum statement. |
| FDCPA | Fair Debt Collection Practices Act (15 U.S.C. § 1692) — federal law governing collection of consumer debts, establishing standards of conduct for debt collectors and rights of consumers. |
| Charity Care | Financial assistance provided to patients who demonstrate financial hardship and who do not qualify for governmental payer programs. Governed by policy FN-FP-004. |
| Authorized Representative | A person legally authorized to make financial decisions on behalf of a patient, including a power of attorney, legal guardian, or healthcare surrogate with financial decision-making authority. |
| Billing Dispute | A formal or informal communication from a patient (or authorized representative) challenging the accuracy, amount, or basis of a charge or balance. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Financial Responsibility Notification at Admission

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | At intake/admission, verify the patient's insurance coverage (Medicare, Medicaid, Medicare Advantage, commercial, self-pay) and document the primary and secondary payer in the billing system. | Intake Coordinator | At admission — before first visit |
| 6.1.2 | Provide the patient or authorized representative with a written Patient Financial Responsibility Statement (Appendix A) that includes: (a) identification of the patient's primary payer; (b) explanation of applicable Medicare home health benefits (no copay/deductible for covered services); (c) identification of any anticipated non-covered services and estimated costs; (d) the agency's billing inquiry contact information; (e) the CMS Home Health Hotline number (1-800-MEDICARE); (f) the patient's right to file a billing grievance; (g) the patient's right to apply for financial assistance. | Intake Coordinator | At admission — before first visit |
| 6.1.3 | The patient or authorized representative shall sign the Financial Responsibility Acknowledgment section of Appendix A. If the patient refuses to sign, document the refusal, the date, and the witness. Refusal to sign does not prevent service delivery. | Intake Coordinator | At admission |
| 6.1.4 | Maintain the signed Financial Responsibility Statement in the patient's billing record. Provide a copy to the patient. | Intake Coordinator | Same day |
| 6.1.5 | If the patient is uninsured or underinsured, screen for Medi-Cal eligibility, Medicare eligibility, and agency charity care eligibility per FN-FP-004 before initiating services. | Intake Coordinator / Billing Coordinator | Within 3 business days of admission |

### 6.2 Advance Beneficiary Notice (ABN) Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Determine whether an ABN is required. An ABN shall be issued when the agency has a genuine, service-specific reason to believe Medicare will not cover a particular item or service. Reasons include: (a) the specific service is not a covered Medicare home health benefit; (b) the patient no longer meets homebound criteria but requests continued services; (c) services ordered exceed what clinical documentation supports as medically necessary; (d) Medicare coverage is expected to end but the patient requests continuation. | Director of Nursing (clinical determination) + Revenue Cycle Director (billing determination) | Before the non-covered service is provided |
| 6.2.2 | Complete the ABN using the current CMS-mandated form (CMS-R-131). The ABN must: (a) identify the specific item(s) or service(s) that may not be covered; (b) state the specific reason(s) Medicare may not pay; (c) provide an estimated cost to the patient for each item/service; (d) be written in plain language (no medical jargon); (e) present the patient with three options clearly. | Revenue Cycle Director / Billing Coordinator (with DON input on clinical reason) | Before the non-covered service |
| 6.2.3 | The three patient options on the ABN are: Option 1: I want the item/service. You may ask to be paid now, but I also want Medicare billed. If Medicare denies payment, I agree to be personally responsible. Option 2: I want the item/service. I want Medicare billed and a written denial so I can appeal. If denied, I agree to be personally responsible. Option 3: I don't want the item/service. I understand Medicare will not be billed. I will not be charged. | Patient selects option on ABN form | At time of ABN delivery |
| 6.2.4 | Deliver the ABN to the patient or authorized representative at least 24 hours before the non-covered service is provided. Verbal-only delivery is insufficient — a signed hard copy is required. | Billing Coordinator or designated clinician | At least 24 hours before service |
| 6.2.5 | The patient (or authorized representative) must sign and date the ABN after selecting one of the three options. If the patient refuses to sign, the agency representative shall: (a) write "Refused to Sign" on the signature line; (b) sign as witness; (c) document the date and circumstances of the refusal. | Patient (signature); Agency representative (witness if refusal) | At time of ABN delivery |
| 6.2.6 | Provide the patient with a copy of the signed ABN. Retain the original in the billing record. | Billing Coordinator | Same day |
| 6.2.7 | Log the ABN in the ABN Issuance Log (Appendix B). | Billing Coordinator | Same day |
| 6.2.8 | ABNs shall NEVER be: (a) issued routinely or as a standard admission form; (b) used as blanket waivers covering all potential services; (c) issued for services that are clearly covered under the Medicare home health benefit; (d) pre-signed in bulk; (e) used to shift financial responsibility to Medicare patients for covered services. Any pattern of routine or blanket ABN issuance shall be investigated by the Compliance Officer as a potential billing or clinical compliance violation. | All personnel | Always |

### 6.3 Notice of Medicare Non-Coverage (NOMNC)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | When Medicare-covered home health services are being terminated (discharge), deliver the NOMNC (CMS Form CMS-10123-NOMNC) to the patient or authorized representative. | Director of Nursing / Case Manager | At least 2 calendar days before the last covered visit |
| 6.3.2 | The NOMNC must identify: (a) the date covered services will end; (b) the reason services are ending; (c) the patient's right to a fast-track appeal through the Quality Improvement Organization (QIO); (d) the QIO contact information. | Director of Nursing | At time of delivery |
| 6.3.3 | Obtain the patient's signature and date on the NOMNC. If the patient refuses to sign, document refusal with date and witness. | Director of Nursing | At time of delivery |
| 6.3.4 | Retain signed NOMNC in the clinical and billing records. | Billing Coordinator / Clinical staff | Same day |

### 6.4 Patient Billing Statements

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Generate monthly billing statements for all patients with an open balance. Statements shall include: (a) patient name and account number; (b) date(s) of service; (c) description of each service; (d) charge for each service; (e) insurance payments applied; (f) adjustments applied; (g) patient balance due; (h) payment due date; (i) accepted payment methods; (j) agency billing inquiry contact name, phone number, and hours of availability. | Billing Coordinator | Monthly — generated within 30 days of service or balance activity |
| 6.4.2 | Deliver statements by mail or, with documented patient consent, by secure electronic delivery. | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 business days of generation |
| 6.4.3 | Respond to all patient billing inquiries within 3 business days of receipt. Document the inquiry, the response, and the resolution. | Billing Coordinator | Within 3 business days |
| 6.4.4 | If a patient requests an itemized statement, provide it within 10 business days. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days of request |

### 6.5 Billing Disputes

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Upon receipt of a patient billing complaint or dispute (verbal or written), log the dispute in the Patient Billing Dispute Log (Appendix C) and acknowledge receipt to the patient within 2 business days. | Billing Coordinator | Log immediately; acknowledge within 2 business days |
| 6.5.2 | Investigate the dispute — review the claim, documentation, remittance, and patient account. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 calendar days of receipt |
| 6.5.3 | Resolve the dispute and communicate the resolution to the patient in writing within 14 calendar days of receipt. If the balance is adjusted, update the patient account. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 14 calendar days |
| 6.5.4 | If the patient is not satisfied with the resolution, inform them of their right to file a grievance per OP-PA-001 and their right to contact Medicare (1-800-MEDICARE) or the QIO. | Revenue Cycle Director | At time of resolution communication |
| 6.5.5 | No collection activity shall be initiated or continued while a billing dispute is pending. | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing |

### 6.6 Patient Collection Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Patient collection shall follow this defined sequence: |  |  |
|  | 30 days: First billing statement (per §6.4.1) | Billing Coordinator | At 30 days from service |
|  | 60 days: Second billing statement with "Past Due" notation and payment request letter | Billing Coordinator | At 60 days |
|  | 90 days: Final notice — written notification that the balance will be referred for collection if not paid or payment arrangement made within 30 days. | Revenue Cycle Director | At 90 days |
|  | 120 days: Collection referral (if balance unresolved, no dispute pending, no charity care application pending) | Revenue Cycle Director + CFO authorization | At 120 days |
| 6.6.2 | Before any collection referral, verify that: (a) no billing dispute is pending; (b) no charity care application is under review per FN-FP-004; (c) no Medicare appeal is open that could affect patient liability; (d) all billing statements were sent to the patient's current address. | Revenue Cycle Director | Before referral |
| 6.6.3 | Collection referral requires written authorization from the Revenue Cycle Director and CFO using the Collection Referral Authorization Form (Appendix D). | Revenue Cycle Director + CFO | Before referral |
| 6.6.4 | Any external collection agency used by the agency shall comply with: (a) FDCPA requirements; (b) California debt collection laws; (c) HIPAA minimum necessary standard (limited to information necessary for collection). The agency shall maintain a current Business Associate Agreement with any collection agency per CO-HP-005. | Revenue Cycle Director / Compliance Officer | Before engagement and ongoing |
| 6.6.5 | Patient balances of $25 or less shall not be referred to external collection. Balances under $25 after 120 days shall be written off as small balance adjustments with Revenue Cycle Director approval. | Revenue Cycle Director | At 120 days |

### 6.7 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ABN issued routinely or as blanket waiver | Revenue Cycle Director and Compliance Officer investigate; potential corrective action for responsible staff | Within 7 calendar days of identification |
| Patient billing dispute not resolved within 14 days | Revenue Cycle Director escalates to CFO | Within 14 calendar days |
| Billing complaint alleging discrimination | Compliance Officer notified; investigation per CO-CP-007 | Within 24 hours |
| Patient unable to pay — self-identifies financial hardship | Screen for charity care per FN-FP-004 before any collection activity | Within 3 business days |
| Collection agency FDCPA violation reported | Compliance Officer investigates; CFO terminates collection agency if violation confirmed | Within 7 calendar days |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Retention |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Financial responsibility notification | Signed Patient Financial Responsibility Statement (Appendix A) | Intake Coordinator | Patient billing record | 7 years |
| ABN issuance | Signed ABN (CMS-R-131) — original | Billing Coordinator | Patient billing record | 7 years |
| ABN log | ABN Issuance Log (Appendix B) | Billing Coordinator | Billing/compliance file | 7 years |
| NOMNC delivery | Signed NOMNC | Director of Nursing | Clinical and billing records | 7 years |
| Billing statements | Copies of all billing statements sent | Billing Coordinator | Billing system | 7 years |
| Billing dispute records | Patient Billing Dispute Log (Appendix C) with investigation and resolution | Revenue Cycle Director | Billing/compliance file | 7 years |
| Collection referral | Collection Referral Authorization Form (Appendix D) | Revenue Cycle Director + CFO | Billing file | 7 years |
| Charity care screening | Screening per FN-FP-004 | Billing Coordinator | Patient billing record | 7 years |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix E | Revenue Cycle Director | Policy acknowledgment file | 7 years |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Financial responsibility statement provided at admission | Audit of patient billing records for signed Appendix A | 100% |
| ABNs issued only when clinically/billing appropriate (not routine) | ABN Issuance Log review; pattern analysis | Zero routine/blanket ABNs |
| ABNs issued before service (not after) | ABN Issuance Log date vs. service date | 100% issued before service |
| ABNs properly completed on CMS-R-131 | Quarterly sample audit of ABN forms | 100% compliance |
| NOMNC delivered ≥2 days before last visit | Clinical record audit | 100% |
| Billing statements sent monthly for all open balances | Billing system report | 100% |
| Billing inquiries responded within 3 business days | Billing inquiry log | 100% |
| Billing disputes resolved within 14 days | Appendix C log | 100% |
| No collection referral with pending dispute or charity care application | Appendix D cross-check with Appendix C and FN-FP-004 | Zero improper referrals |
| Collection agencies compliant with FDCPA | Annual compliance review | 100% |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Financial responsibility information not provided at admission | 42 CFR § 484.50(a)(3) deficiency; patient rights violation | §6.1 mandatory at admission; Appendix A signed |
| ABN issued routinely as admission paperwork | CMS ABN misuse; ABN invalidated — agency cannot bill patient | §6.2.8 strict prohibition; Compliance Officer monitoring |
| ABN issued after service already provided | ABN invalid — agency absorbs cost; cannot bill patient | §6.2.4 minimum 24 hours before service |
| ABN form is outdated version | ABN invalid | Revenue Cycle Director maintains current CMS-R-131 |
| Patient not informed of right to file billing grievance | Patient rights violation | §6.1.2(f) included in Appendix A |
| Collection activity during pending dispute | FDCPA violation; state law violation | §6.5.5 and §6.6.2 prohibition |
| No charity care screening before collection referral | Patient denied access to financial assistance | §6.6.2(b) verification before referral |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All intake coordinators and billing staff shall receive training on this policy within 14 calendar days of hire or assignment. Training shall cover: (a) patient financial responsibility notification requirements; (b) ABN issuance criteria, process, and prohibitions; (c) NOMNC requirements; (d) billing statement standards; (e) billing dispute handling; (f) collection process and FDCPA requirements; (g) charity care screening obligation.
10.2 Annual refresher training on ABN requirements and current CMS ABN form.
10.3 All in-scope personnel shall sign Appendix E within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require CFO review, Compliance Officer review, and Governing Body approval. Re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.
## Appendix A — Patient Financial Responsibility Statement & Acknowledgment
CARE INDEED HOME HEALTH CARE, INC. Patient Financial Responsibility Statement & Acknowledgment Policy Reference: FN-BC-003 | Version: 6.0 | Effective: 2025-07-10
PATIENT INFORMATION

| Field | Entry |
| --- | --- |
| Patient Name | _________________________ |
| Date of Birth | _________________________ |
| Medicare Number (if applicable) | _________________________ |
| Date of Admission | _________________________ |
| Primary Payer | ☐ Medicare Fee-for-Service ☐ Medi-Cal ☐ Medicare Advantage — Plan: _____ ☐ Commercial — Carrier: _____ ☐ Self-Pay ☐ Other: _____ |
| Secondary Payer (if any) | _________________________ |

YOUR MEDICARE HOME HEALTH BENEFITS
If you are a Medicare fee-for-service beneficiary:
Medicare covers skilled nursing, physical therapy, occupational therapy, speech-language pathology, medical social work, and home health aide services when: (1) you are under the care of a physician; (2) you have a documented need for skilled care; (3) you meet the homebound criteria; and (4) the home health agency is Medicare-certified.
There is NO Medicare copayment, coinsurance, or deductible for Medicare-covered home health services.
Medicare does NOT cover: 24-hour nursing care, meals delivered to your home, homemaker/housekeeping services (unless incidental to a covered service), or personal care services when skilled care is not needed.
POTENTIAL PATIENT FINANCIAL OBLIGATIONS

| Item / Service | Covered by Your Insurance? | Estimated Patient Responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Skilled nursing, therapy, aide services (covered home health services) | ☐ Yes — No cost to you (Medicare FFS) ☐ Per plan (Medicare Advantage / Commercial) | $ ________ per your plan |
| Durable Medical Equipment (DME), if ordered | ☐ Yes — Medicare Part B (20% coinsurance may apply) ☐ Per plan ☐ No | $ ________ |
| Non-covered services (if applicable — describe): _________________________ | ☐ No — Patient responsibility | $ ________ |
| Medicare Advantage cost-sharing | ☐ Per your plan benefits | $ ________ per your plan |
| Other: _________________________ |  | $ ________ |

If services are anticipated to NOT be covered by Medicare, you will receive an Advance Beneficiary Notice (ABN) BEFORE those services are provided, giving you the choice to receive or decline the services.
YOUR RIGHTS
You have the right to receive a copy of your itemized bill at any time upon request.
You have the right to file a billing complaint or dispute with the agency. Contact: _________________________ Phone: _____________
You have the right to contact Medicare with questions about your benefits or billing: 1-800-MEDICARE (1-800-633-4227). TTY users call 1-877-486-2048.
You have the right to appeal any Medicare coverage denial.
