# FN-BC-004 — Overpayment Identification & Refund
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-004__ |
| Title | Overpayment Identification & Refund |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims__ |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10__ |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d) (ACA 60-Day Rule), 42 CFR § 401.305 (Reporting and Returning Overpayments), False Claims Act 31 U.S.C. §§ 3729–3733, OIG Compliance Program Guidance, CMS Self-Referral Disclosure Protocol |

## 2. Purpose
Federal law — specifically Section 1128J(d) of the Social Security Act, as added by Section 6402(a) of the Affordable Care Act, and codified at 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d) and implementing regulation 42 CFR § 401.305 — requires Medicare providers to report and return identified overpayments within 60 calendar days of the date the overpayment is identified. Failure to report and return an identified overpayment within this 60-day window creates a "reverse false claim" under the False Claims Act, exposing the agency to treble damages and per-claim penalties of up to $27,894 per violation (2025 adjusted amount). This obligation extends 6 years from the date the overpayment was received.
This policy establishes the mandatory process for identifying, investigating, quantifying, reporting, and returning all Medicare and Medicaid overpayments at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. within full compliance with the 60-day rule. There are no exceptions to this requirement. No amount is too small to report. No business justification exists for retaining a known overpayment.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators, billing specialists, and certified coders
Compliance Officer (overpayment investigation, reporting, 60-day tracking)
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (clinical review of clinically-driven overpayments)
Administrator
Governing Body (notification of material overpayments per GV-GB-001)
Any contractor performing billing, coding, or compliance functions
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall report and return any identified overpayment to Medicare or Medicaid within 60 calendar days of the date the overpayment is identified, without exception, as required by 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d) and 42 CFR § 401.305.
4.2 An overpayment is "identified" when the agency has, or should have through the exercise of reasonable diligence, determined that it has received funds to which it is not entitled under applicable payment rules. The identification date is the date the agency first had credible information creating an obligation to investigate — not the date quantification is completed.
4.3 Upon receipt of any credible information suggesting a potential overpayment, the agency shall commence a reasonable diligence investigation. Reasonable diligence shall include: (a) a timely, good-faith investigation; (b) quantification of the overpayment; and (c) a determination of the cause. Reasonable diligence shall be completed within a maximum of 6 months from receipt of credible information, except in extraordinary circumstances documented by the Compliance Officer.
4.4 The 60-day refund clock begins on the date the agency first received credible information triggering an investigation obligation — even if quantification is not yet complete. The agency shall exercise reasonable diligence within this 60-day window whenever possible, or shall report and refund the estimated amount within 60 days with a final reconciliation to follow.
4.5 No identified overpayment shall be retained, offset against future claims, delayed in refund, or concealed. Deliberate delay, concealment, or failure to return an identified overpayment is a violation of the False Claims Act and this policy, constituting grounds for immediate termination and referral for legal action.
4.6 All identified or suspected overpayments shall be reported to the Compliance Officer immediately upon identification. The Compliance Officer shall maintain a centralized Overpayment Tracking Log (Appendix B) and oversee the investigation and refund process.
4.7 Overpayments exceeding $5,000 individually, or $10,000 in aggregate within any 12-month period, shall be reported to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Any overpayment associated with potential fraud shall be reported to the Governing Body within 48 hours.
4.8 The agency shall consider voluntary self-disclosure to the OIG Self-Disclosure Protocol for any overpayment resulting from potential fraud, kickback, or Stark Law violation. The decision to self-disclose shall be made by the Compliance Officer, CFO, and Administrator in consultation with legal counsel.
4.9 Overpayment identification processes shall be integrated into: (a) monthly payment reconciliation per FN-BC-007; (b) the internal documentation audit program per CO-DC-002; (c) the triangulation audit per CO-DC-004; (d) coding accuracy audits per FN-CM-001/002; (e) denial pattern analysis per FN-BC-002; and (f) the financial compliance monitoring program per FN-FP-007.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Overpayment | Any funds that a person has received or retained under Medicare or Medicaid to which the person, after applicable reconciliation, is not entitled under such program. (42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d)(4)(B)) |
| 60-Day Rule | The requirement under 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d) and 42 CFR § 401.305 that identified overpayments must be reported and returned within 60 calendar days of identification. |
| Identification Date | The date on which the agency has, or should have through reasonable diligence, determined that an overpayment exists. Per CMS, this includes the date the agency receives credible information of a potential overpayment, triggering a duty to investigate with reasonable diligence. |
| Credible Information | Facts, data, allegations, or circumstances that would cause a reasonable person with billing and compliance knowledge to suspect that the agency has received payment to which it is not entitled. Sources include: internal audit findings, staff reports, MAC/RAC/OIG audit results, denial patterns, remittance anomalies, coding audit results, patient complaints, compliance hotline reports, and media/industry alerts. |
| Reasonable Diligence | A timely, good-faith investigation to determine whether an overpayment exists and to quantify the amount. Per 42 CFR § 401.305(a)(2), the obligation to report and return an overpayment exists even if the exact amount has not been determined, as long as reasonable diligence has been exercised. Maximum investigation period: 6 months from receipt of credible information.__ |
| Lookback Period | The period during which the agency must identify and return overpayments. Per 42 CFR § 401.305(f), the lookback period is 6 years from the date the overpayment was received. |
| Reverse False Claim | Liability under the False Claims Act (31 U.S.C. § 3729(a)(1)(G)) arising from knowingly concealing or knowingly and improperly avoiding or decreasing an obligation to pay or transmit money to the federal government — specifically, knowingly retaining a Medicare overpayment beyond the 60-day deadline. |
| Self-Disclosure | Voluntary reporting of compliance concerns (including overpayments resulting from potential fraud, kickback, or Stark violations) to the OIG through the OIG Self-Disclosure Protocol or to the MAC through the CMS voluntary refund process. |
| Statistical Sampling | A methodology for estimating the overpayment amount across a population of claims when the overpayment appears to involve multiple claims, using random sampling and extrapolation per OIG/CMS-accepted statistical methods. |
| MAC Voluntary Refund | The process of reporting and returning an overpayment to the Medicare Administrative Contractor through the voluntary refund submission process, accompanied by a written explanation. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Sources of Overpayment Identification
6.1.1 The Revenue Cycle Director and Compliance Officer shall maintain awareness of all potential sources of overpayment identification. The following are monitored routinely:

| Source | Monitoring Mechanism | Frequency | Responsible Party |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Payment reconciliation reviews | FN-BC-007 monthly reconciliation | Monthly | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Internal documentation audits | CO-DC-002 documentation audit program | Quarterly (minimum__ | Compliance Officer |
| Triangulation audits (clinical/doc/billing alignment) | CO-DC-004 | Quarterly | Compliance Officer |
| Coding accuracy audits (PDGM/ICD-10) | FN-CM-001, FN-CM-002 quarterly audits | Quarterly | Revenue Cycle Director / Coder |
| Denial pattern analysis | FN-BC-002 monthly denial report | Monthly | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Financial compliance monitoring / billing pattern analysis | FN-FP-007 monthly analysis | Monthly | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer |
| MAC/RAC/CERT/ZPIC/UPIC audit findings | Upon receipt per FN-BC-002 §6.5, FN-FP-007 | Upon receipt | Compliance Officer |
| Staff self-reporting / compliance hotline | CO-CP-006 reporting mechanisms | Ongoing (24/7) | Compliance Officer |
| Patient complaints about billing | FN-BC-003 §6.5 | Ongoing | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Remittance advice anomalies | FN-BC-001 §6.3, FN-BC-007 | Monthly | Billing Coordinator |
| QAPI outcome reviews | QA-PI-002, QA-SM-001 | Quarterly | QAPI Coordinator |
| Industry alerts (OIG Work Plan, MAC bulletins) | CO-RA-001 regulatory monitoring | Monthly | Compliance Officer |

### 6.2 Initial Notification and 60-Day Clock Initiation

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Upon identification of credible information suggesting a potential overpayment from ANY source, the identifying person shall notify the Compliance Officer in writing (email or written memo) within 24 hours. The notification shall include: the source of the information, the nature of the suspected overpayment, the patient(s)/episode(s) involved, and the estimated dollar amount (if known). | Any employee / staff member who identifies the information | Within 24 hours of identification |
| 6.2.2 | The Compliance Officer shall log the potential overpayment in the Overpayment Tracking Log (Appendix B) within 24 hours of receiving notification. The log shall record the Identification Date — this is the date the agency first received credible information, which starts the 60-day clock. | Compliance Officer | Within 24 hours |
| 6.2.3 | The Compliance Officer shall calculate the 60-Day Deadline (Identification Date + 60 calendar days) and record it in Appendix B. This deadline is non-negotiable. | Compliance Officer | Same day as logging__ |
| 6.2.__ | The Compliance Officer shall notify the Revenue Cycle Director and CFO in writing within 24 hours of logging, providing the identification date, 60-day deadline, and nature of the suspected overpayment. | Compliance Officer | Within 24 hours of logging |
| 6.2.5 | The Administrator shall be notified within 48 hours. For any suspected__ |  |  |

# FN-BC-004 — Overpayment Identification & Refund
### 6.2 Initial Notification and 60-Day Clock Initiation (Continued)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.5 | The Administrator shall be notified within 48 hours. For any suspected overpayment involving potential fraud, kickback, Stark Law violation, or systematic billing pattern, the Administrator shall be notified within 24 hours and the Governing Body Chair within 48 hours. | Compliance Officer | Within 48 hours (24 hours if fraud-related) |
| 6.2.6 | The Compliance Officer shall determine whether the potential overpayment requires legal counsel engagement. Legal counsel shall be engaged for any overpayment: (a) exceeding $25,000; (b) involving potential fraud or abuse; (c) involving RAC/ZPIC/UPIC investigation; (d) that may require OIG self-disclosure. | Compliance Officer + Administrator | Within 5 calendar days of logging |

### 6.3 Investigation and Quantification (Reasonable Diligence)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | The Compliance Officer shall initiate a reasonable diligence investigation within 3 business days of logging. The investigation shall include: (a) identification of all claims and episodes potentially affected; (b) review of clinical documentation, billing records, coding worksheets, and remittance advice for each affected claim; (c) determination of whether the overpayment resulted from a single isolated error, a recurring pattern, or systemic failure; (d) identification of root cause; (e) quantification of the total overpayment amount. | Compliance Officer (lead); Revenue Cycle Director (billing); Director of Nursing (clinical); Certified Coder (coding) | Investigation initiated within 3 business days of logging |
| 6.3.2 | For isolated single-claim overpayments (e.g., duplicate payment, obvious coding error on one claim), the investigation and quantification shall be completed within 14 calendar days. | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer | Within 14 calendar days |
| 6.3.3 | For multi-claim or potentially systemic overpayments, the Compliance Officer shall determine whether statistical sampling and extrapolation is appropriate. If the potential overpayment involves more than 10 claims with similar error characteristics, the Compliance Officer shall engage a qualified statistical consultant or use CMS/OIG-accepted sampling methodology per RAT-STATS or equivalent. | Compliance Officer | Determination within 14 calendar days; sampling plan developed within 21 calendar days |
| 6.3.4 | The investigation shall determine the lookback period — the timeframe during which the error may have occurred. Per 42 CFR § 401.305(f), the agency must look back 6 years from the date the overpayment was received. The Compliance Officer shall apply the lookback period appropriate to the identified error pattern. | Compliance Officer | As part of investigation |
| 6.3.5 | All investigation activities, findings, calculations, and supporting documentation shall be maintained in the Overpayment Investigation File (Appendix C). The file shall be treated as confidential and may be subject to attorney-client privilege if legal counsel is engaged. | Compliance Officer | Ongoing throughout investigation |
| 6.3.6 | The investigation and quantification shall be completed, and the refund submitted, within the 60-day deadline from the identification date. If full quantification is not possible within 60 days despite reasonable diligence, the agency shall: (a) refund the estimated amount within 60 days; (b) continue the investigation; (c) submit a supplemental refund for any additional amount identified. This approach is supported by CMS guidance but does not extend the 60-day deadline for the initial report and refund. | Compliance Officer + Revenue Cycle Director | Within 60 calendar days of identification date |
| 6.3.7 | The Compliance Officer shall provide weekly status updates to the CFO and Administrator for all open overpayment investigations. Status updates shall include: investigation progress, current estimated amount, 60-day deadline status, and any barriers to completion. | Compliance Officer | Weekly |

### 6.4 Overpayment Reporting and Refund

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Upon completion of quantification (or estimation if full quantification is not yet complete), the Compliance Officer shall prepare the Overpayment Report and Refund Package (Appendix D) containing: (a) written disclosure letter to the MAC identifying the overpayment; (b) reason for the overpayment; (c) affected claims (patient names, Medicare numbers, claim control numbers, dates of service, amounts); (d) total overpayment amount (actual or estimated); (e) methodology used for quantification (including statistical sampling methodology if applicable); (f) root cause of the overpayment; (g) corrective actions implemented or planned to prevent recurrence. | Compliance Officer (preparation); Revenue Cycle Director (billing data); CFO (financial review) | Before 60-day deadline |
| 6.4.2 | The refund shall be submitted to the MAC through the Medicare voluntary refund process using the appropriate method: (a) check submitted with the written disclosure letter for overpayments under $25,000; or (b) CMS-approved electronic refund mechanism if available. The refund submission method shall follow current MAC instructions for the agency's jurisdiction. | Revenue Cycle Director | Before 60-day deadline |
| 6.4.3 | The CFO shall authorize the refund payment. For refunds exceeding $10,000, the Administrator shall also authorize. For refunds exceeding $50,000, the Governing Body shall authorize at a special or regular meeting (whichever is sooner). | CFO (all refunds); Administrator (>$10,000); Governing Body (>$50,000) | Before 60-day deadline |
| 6.4.4 | The Compliance Officer shall confirm receipt of the refund by the MAC within 14 calendar days of submission. If confirmation is not received, the Compliance Officer shall follow up in writing. | Compliance Officer | Within 14 calendar days of submission |
| 6.4.5 | Log the refund completion in the Overpayment Tracking Log (Appendix B) including: refund amount, submission date, MAC confirmation date, and case closure date. | Compliance Officer | Upon completion |
| 6.4.6 | If the overpayment results from potential fraud, kickback, or Stark Law violation, the Compliance Officer, CFO, and Administrator — in consultation with legal counsel — shall determine whether voluntary self-disclosure to the OIG Self-Disclosure Protocol or the CMS Self-Referral Disclosure Protocol is warranted. Self-disclosure decisions shall be documented. | Compliance Officer + CFO + Administrator + Legal Counsel | Within 30 calendar days of identification |

### 6.5 Corrective Action and Recurrence Prevention

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | For every identified overpayment, the Compliance Officer shall complete a Root Cause Analysis (Appendix E) identifying: (a) the specific process, system, or human failure that caused the overpayment; (b) whether the failure was isolated or systemic; (c) contributing factors; (d) the duration of the error. | Compliance Officer | Within 14 calendar days of refund submission |
| 6.5.2 | Based on the root cause analysis, the Compliance Officer shall develop a Corrective Action Plan per QA-AE-003 with: (a) specific corrective actions; (b) responsible parties; (c) implementation deadlines; (d) measurable outcomes to verify effectiveness. | Compliance Officer + affected department head(s) | Within 14 calendar days of RCA completion |
| 6.5.3 | Corrective actions may include but are not limited to: additional staff training; system configuration changes; process redesign; enhanced pre-billing verification steps; additional audit checkpoints; personnel action per HR-ER-002. | As assigned in CAP | Per CAP deadlines |
| 6.5.4 | The Compliance Officer shall monitor corrective action effectiveness at 30, 60, and 90 calendar days post-implementation. If the corrective action is not effective (recurrence detected), the Compliance Officer shall escalate to the Administrator and CFO for enhanced intervention. | Compliance Officer | 30/60/90 days post-implementation |
| 6.5.5 | Overpayment trends shall be reported to the QAPI Committee per QA-PI-002 and the Governing Body in the quarterly compliance report per GV-GB-001 §6.2.3. | Compliance Officer | Quarterly |

### 6.6 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Potential overpayment identified from any source | Report to Compliance Officer in writing | 60-day clock starts; investigation per §6.3 | Within 24 hours |
| Overpayment exceeds $5,000 | Compliance Officer notifies CFO, Administrator, and Revenue Cycle Director | Priority investigation; legal counsel engagement considered | Within 48 hours |
| Overpayment exceeds $25,000 | Compliance Officer notifies CFO, Administrator, and legal counsel | Legal counsel engaged; OIG self-disclosure evaluated | Within 48 hours |
| Overpayment exceeds $50,000 | Governing Body notified | Governing Body authorization for refund; special meeting if necessary | Within 48 hours |
| 60-day deadline approaching (within 14 days) without completed investigation | Compliance Officer escalates to Administrator and CFO | Estimate and refund estimated amount before deadline; continue investigation | Immediately |
| 60-day deadline at risk of being missed | Compliance Officer, CFO, and Administrator emergency conference | Emergency estimated refund submitted; legal counsel notified; incident documented | Within 24 hours |
| Overpayment involves potential fraud, kickback, or Stark violation | Compliance Officer, Administrator, CFO, and legal counsel | Investigation per CO-CP-007; OIG self-disclosure evaluated; affected billing suspended | Within 24 hours |
| Staff member identified as deliberately causing or concealing overpayment | Compliance Officer and HR Director | Immediate suspension from duties; investigation per CO-CP-007 and HR-ER-002; potential law enforcement referral | Within 24 hours |
| MAC/RAC demands repayment | Revenue Cycle Director and Compliance Officer | Verify demand accuracy; pay or appeal per FN-BC-002 | Per MAC demand timeline |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All identified overpayments reported and returned within 60 days | Overpayment Tracking Log (Appendix B) — identification date vs. refund submission date | 100% within 60 calendar days |
| All potential overpayments reported to Compliance Officer within 24 hours | Notification timestamp vs. identification date | 100% |
| Investigation initiated within 3 business days of logging | Appendix B investigation start date | 100% |
| Root cause analysis completed for all overpayments | Appendix E completion rate | 100% |
| Corrective action plans developed for all systemic overpayments | CAP documentation | 100% |
| Corrective action effectiveness measured at 30/60/90 days | Monitoring reports | 100% |
| Governing Body notified of overpayments >$5,000 | Governance meeting minutes | 100% |
| Legal counsel engaged for overpayments >$25,000 | Legal engagement documentation | 100% |
| Overpayment data integrated with QAPI program | QAPI dashboard and committee minutes | Quarterly reporting confirmed |
| Zero 60-day deadline violations | Overpayment Tracking Log | Zero violations |

### 8.2 Surveyor and Auditor Expectations
OIG compliance program auditors will specifically request evidence that the agency has a functioning overpayment identification and refund process. Agencies that cannot demonstrate a formal process risk a finding of inadequate compliance program.
RAC/CERT/UPIC auditors issue overpayment demands; surveyors will verify that the agency responded timely and refunded amounts owed.
CMS surveyors reviewing 42 CFR § 484.100 (compliance with federal laws) will look for evidence that the agency self-identifies and returns overpayments.
OIG auditors will test whether the agency's lookback extends the full 6-year period required by regulation.
DOJ attorneys investigating False Claims Act matters will seek evidence of when the agency first became aware of the overpayment versus when (and if) it was returned.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Overpayment identified but not reported to Compliance Officer | 60-day clock running without agency tracking; potential reverse false claim | §6.2.1 mandatory 24-hour notification |
| Investigation delayed beyond 60-day window | Reverse false claim liability; FCA treble damages | §6.3.6 estimated refund within 60 days; weekly status updates |
| 60-day clock start date misdetermined (using quantification date instead of identification date) | Deadline miscalculated; late refund creates FCA liability | §4.2 and §4.4 clear definition of identification date |
| Overpayment amount underestimated in initial refund | Obligation for supplemental refund; potential bad faith finding | §6.3.6 supplemental refund process |
| No lookback beyond single claim | Systemic overpayment continues unreported for years | §6.3.4 mandatory 6-year lookback assessment |
| Overpayment caused by fraud not referred to OIG | Missed self-disclosure opportunity; enhanced penalties | §6.4.6 self-disclosure evaluation |
| No corrective action to prevent recurrence | Same error generates additional overpayments | §6.5 mandatory RCA and CAP |
| Staff afraid to report potential overpayments | Overpayments go unreported; FCA liability accumulates | CO-CP-005 non-retaliation; CO-CP-006 reporting mechanisms |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All billing coordinators, coding personnel, and the Revenue Cycle Director shall receive initial training on the 60-day overpayment rule within 14 calendar days of hire or assignment to billing functions. Training shall be conducted by the Compliance Officer and must cover: (a) the legal basis of the 60-day rule (42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d), 42 CFR § 401.305); (b) the definition of "identified" and "identification date"; (c) the reasonable diligence standard; (d) the 6-year lookback period; (e) the reverse false claim consequences of failure to report and return; (f) the obligation and process for reporting potential overpayments to the Compliance Officer within 24 hours; (g) prohibited conduct (retention, concealment, delay); (h) the investigation and refund process under this policy.
10.2 The Compliance Officer shall provide annual refresher training to all billing, coding, and revenue cycle personnel, plus the Director of Nursing, Administrator, and CFO. Annual training shall include: current penalty amounts, recent enforcement actions, and any CMS/OIG guidance updates.
10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix F) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new hire/assignment.
10.4 The Compliance Officer shall maintain a training tracking log. Training non-compliance shall be reported to the CFO and HR Director within 7 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the current approved version is valid. Superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require: (a) Compliance Officer and CFO review; (b) Governing Body approval documented in meeting minutes; (c) re-acknowledgment by all in-scope personnel within 14 calendar days; (d) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the CFO with notification to the Compliance Officer and Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

# FN-BC-005 — Pre-Claim Review Compliance
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-005 |
| Title | Pre-Claim Review Compliance |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Triggered |
| Next Review Date | Triggered — upon CMS PCR program implementation or regulatory change |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | Section 1115A of the Social Security Act (CMS Innovation Center), 42 CFR Part 424, CMS Pre-Claim Review Demonstration for Home Health Services, CMS HHVBP, 42 CFR § 484.100 |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's readiness framework and operational procedures for compliance with any CMS Pre-Claim Review (PCR) demonstration program applicable to home health services in California or any other state in which Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. operates. CMS has implemented PCR demonstrations in selected states to reduce improper payments in the home health industry. While PCR may not be currently active in all jurisdictions, CMS retains the authority to expand or reinstate PCR programs at any time. This policy ensures Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. has a documented, operationally ready process for immediate PCR compliance upon CMS activation, minimizing disruption to patient care and revenue cycle operations.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
Compliance Officer
Administrator
All certified coders
All clinical documentation staff supporting PCR submissions
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a state of operational readiness for PCR compliance such that the agency can implement full PCR submission within 30 calendar days of CMS announcement of a PCR program affecting the agency's service area.
4.2 When a PCR program is active and applicable to the agency, all non-excepted home health episodes shall be submitted for pre-claim review prior to final claim submission. No final claim shall be submitted for a PCR-required episode without a provisional affirmation or affirmation from the PCR reviewer.
4.3 The Revenue Cycle Director shall designate a PCR Coordinator responsible for monitoring CMS PCR announcements, managing PCR submissions, tracking decisions, and managing resubmissions and appeals.
4.4 PCR submissions shall contain all required documentation as specified by the CMS PCR program requirements, which typically include: (a) a completed PCR request form; (b) the Plan of Care (485); (c) physician certification; (d) face-to-face encounter documentation; (e) OASIS assessment; (f) clinical documentation supporting medical necessity, homebound status, and skilled care need.
4.5 The agency shall maintain a PCR Decision Tracking Log for all submitted episodes and shall not bill any episode where the PCR determination is "non-affirmation" unless the non-affirmation is successfully appealed through the PCR reconsideration process.
4.6 Non-affirmation of a PCR request does not prohibit the agency from providing or continuing services if clinically necessary, but it does prevent Medicare billing until the decision is overturned or the underlying documentation deficiency is corrected and the request is resubmitted.
4.7 The Compliance Officer shall monitor PCR non-affirmation rates and patterns monthly. A non-affirmation rate exceeding 10% shall trigger a root cause analysis within 30 calendar days.
4.8 All PCR-related processes shall be integrated with the agency's pre-billing verification (FN-BC-001 §6.1), documentation standards (CL-CD-001 through CL-CD-004), and the corporate compliance program (CO-CP-001).
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Pre-Claim Review (PCR) | A CMS demonstration program requiring home health agencies in designated states to submit documentation supporting Medicare home health episodes to a CMS review contractor for evaluation before the final claim can be submitted for payment. |
| Affirmation | A PCR determination that the submitted documentation supports Medicare coverage criteria. The agency may proceed with final claim submission. |
| Provisional Affirmation | An affirmation issued when the documentation substantially supports coverage but contains minor deficiencies that the agency certifies will be corrected. The agency may proceed with billing but must correct deficiencies. |
| Non-Affirmation | A PCR determination that the submitted documentation does not support Medicare coverage criteria. The agency may not submit a final claim for the non-affirmed episode unless the decision is overturned on resubmission or appeal. A non-affirmation is NOT a denial and does not create appeal rights under the standard Medicare appeals process — it has its own reconsideration process. |
| Resubmission | The process of correcting documentation deficiencies identified in a non-affirmation and resubmitting the PCR request for a new determination. |
| PCR Reconsideration | The review process available when the agency disagrees with a non-affirmation determination. |
| Excepted Episodes | Episodes that are exempt from PCR requirements per CMS designation, which may include certain episode types or circumstances. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 PCR Readiness Monitoring

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | The Compliance Officer shall monitor CMS Federal Register notices, MLN announcements, and MAC bulletins for any PCR program activation, expansion, or modification affecting California or any state in which the agency operates. | Compliance Officer | Monthly — per CO-RA-001 |
| 6.1.2 | Upon identification of a PCR program announcement applicable to the agency, the Compliance Officer shall notify the Administrator, CFO, Revenue Cycle Director, and Director of Nursing within 48 hours and initiate the PCR Implementation Checklist (Appendix A). | Compliance Officer | Within 48 hours of announcement |
| 6.1.3 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall designate a PCR Coordinator within 7 calendar days of notification. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 7 calendar days |
| 6.1.4 | The PCR Coordinator shall complete staff training per §10 within 30 calendar days of designation. | PCR Coordinator | Within 30 calendar days |

### 6.2 PCR Submission Process (When PCR Program Is Active)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | For every new home health episode (SOC, ROC, or Recertification) that is not excepted from PCR requirements, the PCR Coordinator shall initiate the PCR submission process by compiling the required documentation package. | PCR Coordinator | Within 5 calendar days of SOC/ROC/Recert date |
| 6.2.2 | The required PCR documentation package shall include all items specified by the active CMS PCR program, which typically include: (a) PCR request form (per CMS specification); (b) signed Plan of Care (485) with valid certification; (c) face-to-face encounter documentation; (d) OASIS assessment; (e) clinical notes demonstrating homebound status, skilled care need, and medical necessity; (f) physician orders. | PCR Coordinator (compilation); Director of Nursing (clinical documentation verification); Revenue Cycle Director (billing elements) | Compiled within 5 calendar days of episode start |
| 6.2.3 | The Director of Nursing (or clinical designee) shall review the clinical documentation package for completeness and quality before PCR submission. The clinical review shall verify: (a) homebound status narrative is specific and compliant; (b) skilled care need is clearly documented; (c) medical necessity is supported; (d) OASIS is complete and consistent; (e) physician certification is signed and dated appropriately; (f) F2F documentation is present and valid. | Director of Nursing / Clinical Designee | Within 2 business days of package compilation |
| 6.2.4 | Submit the PCR request to the designated CMS review contractor via the method specified by the PCR program (typically electronic portal). | PCR Coordinator | Within the CMS-specified submission window (per program rules) |
| 6.2.5 | Log the submission in the PCR Decision Tracking Log (Appendix B) including: patient name, Medicare number, episode dates, submission date, and documentation included. | PCR Coordinator | Same day as submission |
| 6.2.6 | Monitor for PCR determination (affirmation, provisional affirmation, or non-affirmation) within the CMS-specified response window (typically 10–15 business days). | PCR Coordinator | Per CMS timeline |

### 6.3 PCR Decision Processing

| Decision | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Affirmation | Log outcome in Appendix B. Proceed with final claim submission per FN-BC-001. Ensure claim is submitted within timely filing deadline. | PCR Coordinator / Billing Coordinator | Per FN-BC-001 |
| Provisional Affirmation | Log outcome in Appendix B. Correct identified deficiencies within the CMS-specified timeframe. Proceed with final claim submission. Document corrections made. | PCR Coordinator / Director of Nursing | Per CMS timeframe |
| Non-Affirmation | Log outcome in Appendix B. DO NOT submit final claim. Initiate non-affirmation response per §6.4. | PCR Coordinator | Within 24 hours of non-affirmation receipt |

### 6.4 Non-Affirmation Response

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Upon receipt of a non-affirmation, the PCR Coordinator shall log the non-affirmation reason(s) in Appendix B and immediately notify the Revenue Cycle Director and Director of Nursing. | PCR Coordinator | Within 24 hours |
| 6.4.2 | The Director of Nursing and Revenue Cycle Director shall jointly review the non-affirmation reason(s) and determine whether: (a) the documentation can be corrected/supplemented and the request resubmitted; (b) the non-affirmation should be challenged through the reconsideration process; or (c) the non-affirmation is valid and no further action is appropriate. | Director of Nursing + Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days |
| 6.4.3 | If resubmission is appropriate, the Director of Nursing shall ensure the clinical documentation deficiencies are corrected (without fabrication, backdating, or alteration of original records — corrections per CO-DC-003). The PCR Coordinator shall compile and resubmit the updated package within the CMS-specified resubmission window. | Director of Nursing (clinical correction); PCR Coordinator (resubmission) | Within CMS resubmission deadline |
| 6.4.4 | If reconsideration is appropriate, the PCR Coordinator shall file a reconsideration request per the CMS PCR program reconsideration process, including a written argument addressing each non-affirmation reason. | PCR Coordinator + Director of Nursing | Within CMS reconsideration deadline |
| 6.4.5 | If the non-affirmation is accepted as valid: (a) the episode shall not be billed to Medicare; (b) the episode shall be logged as a billing loss; (c) root cause analysis per §6.6 shall be initiated; (d) the patient shall NOT be held financially responsible unless a valid ABN was issued per FN-BC-003. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 business days of decision |

### 6.5 Patient Care Continuity During PCR

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | PCR non-affirmation does NOT require the agency to stop providing clinically necessary services. The decision to continue or discontinue services shall be based solely on clinical need as determined by the Director of Nursing. | Director of Nursing | Ongoing |
| 6.5.2 | If services continue during a non-affirmed episode, the Director of Nursing shall ensure that the clinical documentation clearly supports the clinical need for continued services regardless of payer status. | Director of Nursing | Ongoing |
| 6.5.3 | If services are continued without Medicare coverage, the patient must be notified of potential financial responsibility and an ABN issued per FN-BC-003 if applicable. | Revenue Cycle Director / Director of Nursing | Before continued services |

### 6.6 PCR Trending and Root Cause Analysis

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | The PCR Coordinator shall produce a Monthly PCR Performance Report (Appendix C) including: total submissions, affirmation rate, provisional affirmation rate, non-affirmation rate, resubmission rate, resubmission success rate, reconsideration rate, reconsideration success rate, top non-affirmation reasons, and trending. | PCR Coordinator | Monthly — by 15th of following month |
| 6.6.2 | If the non-affirmation rate exceeds 10% in any calendar month, the Compliance Officer shall initiate a root cause analysis per QA-AE-002 within 30 calendar days. | Compliance Officer | Within 30 calendar days of trigger |
| 6.6.3 | PCR performance data shall be integrated with the QAPI dashboard per QA-PI-002 and reported to the Governing Body in the quarterly financial/compliance report. | QAPI Coordinator / Revenue Cycle Director | Quarterly |

### 6.7 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CMS announces PCR activation affecting agency | Compliance Officer notifies Administrator, CFO, RCD, DON | Within 48 hours |
| Non-affirmation rate exceeds 10% | Compliance Officer and Revenue Cycle Director initiate RCA | Within 30 calendar days |
| Non-affirmation rate exceeds 20% | CFO and Administrator notified; emergency corrective action | Within 7 calendar days |
| Clinical documentation deficiencies are primary non-affirmation driver | Director of Nursing implements targeted clinical documentation training | Within 14 calendar days |
| Resubmission deadline at risk | PCR Coordinator escalates to Revenue Cycle Director and DON | Immediately |
| Patient care continuity concern due to PCR non-affirmation | Director of Nursing determines clinical necessity independently of PCR status | Immediately |

| Non-affirmation rate | Appendix C monthly report | ≤ 10% |
| Resubmission filed for all correctable non-affirmations | Appendix B resubmission tracking | 100% |
| No final claims submitted for non-affirmed episodes | Cross-check Appendix B vs. claims submission log | Zero violations |

| Agency unaware of PCR program activation | Non-compliance from day one; all claims at risk | §6.1.1 monthly CMS monitoring |
| Documentation package incomplete at PCR submission | Non-affirmation; billing delay | §6.2.3 clinical review before submission |
| Final claim submitted for non-affirmed episode | Improper billing; potential False Claims Act exposure | §4.2 prohibition; Appendix B tracking |
| Homebound status documentation too generic | Top non-affirmation reason nationally | CL-CA-005; clinical training |
| F2F encounter documentation missing or expired | Non-affirmation; cannot cure retrospectively | CL-CA-006 and CL-CA-007 tracking |
| Resubmission deadline missed | Episode lost; revenue impact | §6.4.3 deadline tracking |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 When a PCR program is activated, all billing, coding, and clinical documentation staff shall receive PCR-specific training within 30 calendar days of program activation. Training shall cover: (a) PCR program requirements and submission process; (b) required documentation elements; (c) clinical documentation quality standards specific to PCR; (d) non-affirmation response procedures; (e) the prohibition on billing non-affirmed episodes; (f) patient care continuity obligations.
10.2 The Director of Nursing shall conduct targeted clinical documentation training focused on homebound status, medical necessity, and skilled care need documentation — the three most common PCR non-affirmation drivers nationally.
10.3 Annual refresher training while PCR is active, including review of non-affirmation patterns and corrective actions.
10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign Appendix D within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per EN-LC-001. This policy has a Triggered review cycle — reviewed upon CMS PCR program activation, modification, or termination. Substantive revisions require CFO and Compliance Officer review, Governing Body approval, and re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.

# FN-BC-006 — Request for Anticipated Payment (RAP) / Notice of Admission (NOA) Management
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-006 |
| Title | Request for Anticipated Payment (RAP) / Notice of Admission (NOA) Management |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR Part 424, 42 CFR § 484.205 (Home Health Prospective Payment System), CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10, CMS Final Rule CY 2022 HH PPS (86 FR 62240) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes standards for timely and accurate submission of the Notice of Admission (NOA) to Medicare for all home health episodes at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Effective January 1, 2022, CMS replaced the Request for Anticipated Payment (RAP) with the NOA under the Patient-Driven Groupings Model (PDGM). The NOA is a one-time electronic notification submitted to the Medicare Administrative Contractor (MAC) within 5 calendar days of the start of care (SOC) date that notifies Medicare of the patient's admission to home health services. Unlike the former RAP, the NOA does not generate a payment — it establishes the episode in the Medicare payment system and is a prerequisite for final claim submission. Failure to submit a timely NOA results in a reduction of the final payment for the affected 30-day period. This policy ensures 100% compliance with NOA submission requirements to prevent payment reductions and ensure uninterrupted revenue cycle operations.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (SOC date coordination)
Compliance Officer
Intake Coordinators (admission date tracking)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall submit a Notice of Admission (NOA) to the MAC within 5 calendar days of the start of care date for every new Medicare home health episode (SOC) and every Resumption of Care (ROC) episode, as required by CMS.
4.2 The NOA is a one-time submission per beneficiary per period of care. It is NOT required for subsequent 30-day periods within the same period of care (recertifications within a continuous episode) unless the patient is readmitted after a discharge.
4.3 If the NOA is submitted after the 5-calendar-day window but within 30 calendar days of the SOC/ROC date, the final payment for the first 30-day period is reduced by a percentage amount established by CMS (currently 1/30th of the full payment amount for each day the NOA is late).
4.4 If the NOA is not submitted within 30 calendar days of the SOC/ROC date, the episode is not established in the Medicare system and no payment can be made for the first 30-day period. This constitutes a permanent revenue loss.
4.5 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain an NOA Submission Tracking Dashboard monitoring: (a) SOC/ROC dates; (b) NOA submission dates; (c) NOA acceptance confirmation; (d) timely submission compliance rate.
4.6 The Billing Coordinator shall confirm NOA acceptance by the MAC through the clearinghouse acknowledgment (999/277CA transactions) within 2 business days of submission.
4.7 NOA submission requires the following minimum data elements: (a) Medicare beneficiary number; (b) provider NPI and CCN; (c) SOC or ROC date; (d) type of admission (SOC vs. ROC); (e) physician NPI. The agency shall ensure all required data elements are accurate at the time of submission.
4.8 The NOA submission process shall be tightly integrated with the intake/admission workflow so that the NOA can be submitted within 24 hours of SOC whenever possible, with a maximum of 5 calendar days.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| NOA | Notice of Admission — an electronic notification submitted to the MAC within 5 calendar days of the SOC date to establish the home health episode in the Medicare payment system. Replaced the RAP effective January 1, 2022. Does not generate a payment. |
| RAP | Request for Anticipated Payment — the former Medicare home health payment mechanism (retired December 31, 2021) that provided an initial split-percentage payment upon request. Replaced by the NOA under PDGM. This policy uses "NOA" exclusively but retains "RAP" in the title for historical reference and search compatibility. |
| SOC Date | Start of Care date — the date of the first billable visit in a new home health episode. |
| ROC Date | Resumption of Care date — the date of the first billable visit following a qualifying Inpatient Facility Stay during an existing home health episode. |
| Period of Care | The continuous period during which a patient receives home health services from the agency, from SOC through discharge. May include multiple 30-day payment periods and multiple certification/recertification periods. |
| Timely NOA | An NOA submitted within 5 calendar days of the SOC or ROC date. |
| Late NOA | An NOA submitted after day 5 but within 30 calendar days of the SOC/ROC date. Results in a payment reduction. |
| Missed NOA | An NOA not submitted within 30 calendar days of the SOC/ROC date. Results in permanent loss of payment for the first 30-day period. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 NOA Submission Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Upon completion of the first billable visit (SOC or ROC), the clinical staff shall confirm the SOC/ROC date in the EHR and notify the billing department that the visit has been completed. | Admitting Clinician / Director of Nursing | Same day as first visit |
| 6.1.2 | The Billing Coordinator shall verify the following data elements for NOA submission: (a) patient Medicare number — validated through eligibility verification; (b) correct SOC or ROC date; (c) agency NPI and CCN; (d) attending physician NPI; (e) type of admission (SOC or ROC). | Billing Coordinator | Within 24 hours of first visit |
| 6.1.3 | Submit the NOA electronically to the MAC through the agency's designated clearinghouse using the appropriate electronic transaction format per CMS specifications. | Billing Coordinator | Within 24 hours of data verification — never later than day 5 from SOC/ROC |
| 6.1.4 | Log the NOA submission in the NOA Submission Tracking Log (Appendix A) including: patient name, Medicare number, SOC/ROC date, NOA submission date, days from SOC to submission, and submission status. | Billing Coordinator | Same day as submission |
| 6.1.5 | Confirm clearinghouse and MAC acceptance of the NOA through 999/277CA transaction acknowledgments within 2 business days. If the NOA is rejected, identify the rejection reason, correct the error, and resubmit immediately. | Billing Coordinator | Within 2 business days |
| 6.1.6 | Log the acceptance confirmation in Appendix A. If rejected and resubmitted, log the rejection reason, correction, resubmission date, and final acceptance date. | Billing Coordinator | Same day as confirmation |

### 6.2 Timeliness Monitoring and Escalation

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall review the NOA Submission Tracking Log (Appendix A) daily to identify any SOC/ROC dates that are approaching the 5-day submission deadline without a submitted NOA. | Revenue Cycle Director | Daily |
| 6.2.2 | Any episode at day 3 without NOA submission shall be flagged as "approaching deadline" and the Billing Coordinator shall be notified in writing to prioritize submission. | Revenue Cycle Director | At day 3 |
| 6.2.3 | Any episode at day 5 without NOA submission shall trigger immediate escalation to the Revenue Cycle Director for same-day resolution. | Revenue Cycle Director | At day 5 — same day resolution required |
| 6.2.4 | Any episode that exceeds day 5 without NOA submission (late NOA) shall be: (a) submitted immediately; (b) logged as a late submission with the number of days late; (c) the expected payment reduction calculated and documented; (d) root cause documented. | Revenue Cycle Director + Billing Coordinator | Immediately upon identification |
| 6.2.5 | Any episode at day 25 without NOA submission shall trigger written escalation to the CFO and Administrator as a critical revenue loss risk. | Revenue Cycle Director | At day 25 |
| 6.2.6 | Any episode that exceeds day 30 without NOA submission (missed NOA) shall be: (a) documented as a permanent billing loss; (b) root cause analyzed; (c) reported to the CFO as a revenue loss event; (d) the responsible process failure corrected per §6.4. | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer | Within 24 hours of deadline expiration |

### 6.3 NOA for Specific Scenarios

| Scenario | NOA Requirement | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| New SOC (first admission) | NOA required within 5 days of first visit | Standard process per §6.1 |
| Resumption of Care (ROC) following inpatient stay | NOA required within 5 days of ROC date (first visit after resumption) | Billing Coordinator submits new NOA with ROC date |
| Recertification (continued episode, no discharge) | No new NOA required — NOA is one-time per period of care | No action — document in Appendix A that NOA was previously submitted |
| Patient readmitted after discharge | New NOA required — new period of care | Standard process per §6.1 |
| Patient transferred to another HHA | Receiving HHA responsible for their own NOA | Care Indeed submits discharge claim for its episodes |
| NOA rejected by MAC | Correct error and resubmit immediately | Per §6.1.5 |

### 6.4 Root Cause Analysis for Late or Missed NOAs

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | For every late NOA (submitted after day 5) or missed NOA (not submitted within 30 days), the Revenue Cycle Director shall document the root cause and implement immediate corrective action. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 7 calendar days |
| 6.4.2 | Common root causes to investigate: (a) delayed SOC date notification from clinical staff; (b) eligibility verification delays; (c) incorrect Medicare number; (d) billing staff workload or oversight; (e) system or clearinghouse issues; (f) ROC not properly identified. | Revenue Cycle Director | During investigation |
| 6.4.3 | If late or missed NOAs occur more than twice in any 90-day period, the Revenue Cycle Director shall implement a systemic process improvement and report to the CFO. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 30 calendar days of trigger |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NOA submitted within 5 days of SOC/ROC | Appendix A: SOC date vs. submission date | 100% |
| NOA accepted by MAC (no rejections unresolved) | 999/277CA confirmations | 100% |
| Zero missed NOAs (beyond 30 days) | Appendix A audit | Zero |
| Late NOA root cause documented | RCA documentation | 100% for all late NOAs |
| NOA payment reductions | Financial impact tracking | $0 (target: zero late submissions) |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Clinical staff does not notify billing of SOC date timely | NOA submission delayed; payment reduction or loss | §6.1.1 same-day notification; §6.2 daily monitoring |
| Medicare number incorrect | NOA rejection; resubmission delays | §6.1.2 eligibility verification before submission |
| ROC not identified as requiring new NOA | Missed NOA for ROC episode | §6.3 scenario table; staff training |
| System or clearinghouse downtime | Submission delayed | Backup submission process; escalation per §6.2 |
| Billing staff workload causes oversight | Late or missed submissions | Daily dashboard review per §6.2.1 |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All billing coordinators and the Revenue Cycle Director shall receive training on NOA requirements within 14 calendar days of hire. Training shall cover: (a) NOA vs. RAP distinction; (b) the 5-day submission deadline; (c) payment reduction consequences for late submission; (d) permanent payment loss for missed submission; (e) NOA data element requirements; (f) submission and confirmation process; (g) escalation protocols.
10.2 All intake coordinators and admitting clinicians shall receive training on their obligation to notify the billing department of the SOC/ROC date on the same day as the first visit.
10.3 Annual refresher training for all billing staff.
10.4 All in-scope personnel shall sign Appendix B within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require CFO review and Governing Body approval. Re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.

# FN-BC-007 — Payment & Reimbursement Reconciliation
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-BC-007 |
| Title | Payment & Reimbursement Reconciliation |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | BC — Billing & Claims |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR Part 424, 42 CFR § 484.100, 42 CFR § 484.105, 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d) (60-Day Rule), CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10, GAAP/FASB Revenue Recognition Standards |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the mandatory process for reconciling expected Medicare and other payer reimbursements against actual payments received by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Payment reconciliation is a critical internal control that ensures: (a) the agency receives the correct PDGM payment for every billed 30-day period; (b) underpayments are identified and pursued; (c) overpayments are identified and reported/returned within 60 days per FN-BC-004 and 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7k(d); (d) payment anomalies are investigated for root cause; (e) revenue cycle performance is accurately measured and reported to agency leadership and the Governing Body. Without systematic reconciliation, the agency cannot detect payment errors — whether in the agency's favor (creating overpayment liability) or against the agency (creating revenue loss). Both scenarios carry financial, regulatory, and compliance consequences.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators and billing specialists
Compliance Officer (overpayment identification linkage)
Administrator
QAPI Coordinator (revenue cycle performance integration)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall perform payment reconciliation for 100% of Medicare claims within 30 calendar days of payment receipt (remittance advice date).
4.2 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a Payment Reconciliation Worksheet (Appendix A) for every 30-day payment period comparing: (a) expected PDGM payment (based on the claim's PDGM classification — clinical grouping, functional level, comorbidity adjustment, timing, and admission source); (b) actual payment received per the Medicare Remittance Advice (835 transaction); (c) any adjustments, offsets, or sequestration reductions applied.
4.3 Any payment discrepancy exceeding $100 between the expected PDGM payment and the actual payment received shall be investigated within 10 business days. The investigation shall determine whether the discrepancy is due to: (a) correct CMS adjustment (sequestration, outlier, LUPA, PEP); (b) agency coding or billing error; (c) MAC processing error; (d) overpayment to the agency; or (e) underpayment to the agency.
4.4 Identified underpayments shall be pursued through the appropriate channel: (a) MAC inquiry for processing errors; (b) appeal per FN-BC-002 for denied amounts; (c) replacement claim for agency billing errors.
4.5 Identified overpayments shall be immediately referred to the Compliance Officer for processing per FN-BC-004 (60-Day Overpayment Rule).
4.6 The Revenue Cycle Director shall produce a Monthly Payment Reconciliation Summary Report (Appendix B) by the 20th of each month covering the prior month's payments. The report shall be reviewed by the CFO within 5 business days.
4.7 Quarterly payment reconciliation data shall be included in the financial report to the Governing Body per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.
4.8 Non-Medicare payer payments (Medicare Advantage, Medi-Cal, commercial) shall also be reconciled monthly against contracted rates or fee schedules per FN-FP-001.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Payment Reconciliation | The process of comparing expected reimbursement against actual payment received for each billed claim, identifying discrepancies, and resolving variances. |
| Expected PDGM Payment | The payment amount the agency anticipates receiving based on the PDGM classification of the 30-day payment period, calculated using the current CMS PDGM payment rates, case-mix weights, and applicable wage index for the agency's location. |
| Actual Payment | The payment amount received from the MAC as documented on the Medicare Remittance Advice (835 transaction). |
| Payment Variance | The difference between expected payment and actual payment. Positive variance = overpayment to agency. Negative variance = underpayment to agency. |
| Sequestration | Mandatory across-the-board reduction in Medicare payments required by the Budget Control Act. Currently applied as a percentage reduction to all Medicare FFS claims. |
| LUPA | Low Utilization Payment Adjustment — a per-visit payment applied when the number of visits in a 30-day period falls below the PDGM LUPA threshold for the applicable payment group. Replaces the full PDGM episode payment. |
| PEP | Partial Episode Payment — a payment adjustment applied when a patient is transferred to another HHA or discharged and readmitted to the same HHA within a 30-day period. |
| Outlier Payment | An additional payment beyond the standard PDGM amount for episodes with unusually high costs, calculated by CMS based on a fixed-dollar loss threshold and loss-sharing ratio. |
| 835 Transaction | The HIPAA-standard electronic remittance advice transaction used by Medicare to communicate payment, adjustment, and denial information. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Payment Receipt and Initial Processing

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Upon receipt of each Medicare Remittance Advice (835 transaction), the Billing Coordinator shall download and import the remittance data into the billing system. | Billing Coordinator | Within 1 business day of receipt |
| 6.1.2 | The Billing Coordinator shall verify that each claim listed on the remittance matches a submitted claim in the Claims Submission Log (FN-BC-001 Appendix C). Any claim on the remittance that does not match the submission log shall be flagged for investigation. | Billing Coordinator | Within 2 business days |
| 6.1.3 | Post all payments, adjustments, denials, and offsets to the patient accounts in the billing system. | Billing Coordinator | Within 2 business days |
| 6.1.4 | Route all denied claims to the denial management process per FN-BC-002. | Billing Coordinator | Same day as posting |

### 6.2 Payment Reconciliation Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | For each paid claim on the remittance, complete the Payment Reconciliation Worksheet (Appendix A). The worksheet shall document: (a) patient name and Medicare number; (b) 30-day period dates; (c) PDGM payment group (clinical grouping, functional level, comorbidity adjustment, timing, admission source); (d) expected PDGM payment amount (calculated using current CMS rate tables and wage index); (e) actual payment amount per 835; (f) sequestration reduction amount; (g) any other adjustments (LUPA, PEP, outlier); (h) net payment variance (actual minus expected after accounting for known adjustments). | Revenue Cycle Director / Billing Coordinator | Within 10 business days of remittance receipt |
| 6.2.2 | Calculate the expected PDGM payment using the following: Base Payment Rate × Case-Mix Weight × Wage Index Adjustment = Expected Payment. Apply sequestration reduction percentage. Apply any applicable LUPA, PEP, or outlier adjustment. | Revenue Cycle Director / Certified Coder | As part of reconciliation |
| 6.2.3 | Compare actual payment to expected payment. If the net variance is within $100 (positive or negative), the claim is reconciled — log as "Reconciled" in Appendix A. | Revenue Cycle Director | Per claim |
| 6.2.4 | If the net variance exceeds $100 (positive or negative), the claim requires investigation per §6.3. Log as "Variance — Investigation Required" in Appendix A. | Revenue Cycle Director | Per claim |

### 6.3 Variance Investigation

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | For each claim with a variance exceeding $100, investigate the cause. Common variance causes include: | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days of identification |


| Variance Type | Common Cause | Investigation Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Underpayment — lower than expected | MAC applied different PDGM group than billed; LUPA applied unexpectedly; PEP applied; sequestration rate changed; wage index adjustment | Verify PDGM classification on claim vs. MAC determination on 835; check for LUPA/PEP indicators; verify current sequestration rate |
| Underpayment — partial payment | Portion of claim denied; adjustment applied | Review adjustment reason codes on 835; route denied portion to FN-BC-002 |
| Overpayment — higher than expected | Duplicate payment; MAC applied higher PDGM group; outlier payment received; prior adjustment reversed | Verify no duplicate claim; verify PDGM classification; check for outlier indicator; review prior adjustments |
| Zero payment | Full denial | Route to FN-BC-002 |
| Offset applied | Prior overpayment recouped; demand letter offset | Verify offset against known overpayment or demand; cross-reference FN-BC-004 |


| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.2 | Document the investigation findings in the Variance Investigation section of Appendix A. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days |
| 6.3.3 | Based on investigation findings, take the appropriate action: | Revenue Cycle Director | Per finding |


| Finding | Action | Responsible Party |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MAC processing error (underpayment) | Contact MAC for correction or file appeal per FN-BC-002 | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Agency billing/coding error (underpayment) | Submit corrected/replacement claim per FN-BC-001 §6.2.6 | Billing Coordinator |
| Agency coding error (overpayment) | Report to Compliance Officer per FN-BC-004 | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Duplicate payment (overpayment) | Report to Compliance Officer per FN-BC-004 | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Correct CMS adjustment (sequestration, LUPA, PEP, outlier) | Log as reconciled with explanation | Revenue Cycle Director |
| Offset for prior overpayment/demand | Verify against FN-BC-004 log; log as reconciled | Revenue Cycle Director |

| 6.3.4 | All identified overpayments shall be reported to the Compliance Officer within 24 hours per FN-BC-004 §6.2.1. The 60-day clock starts upon identification. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 24 hours | | 6.3.5 | All identified underpayments shall be pursued within 30 calendar days. If MAC correction is not received within 60 calendar days, escalate to CFO. | Revenue Cycle Director | Pursuit within 30 days; escalation at 60 days |
### 6.4 Monthly Reconciliation Summary

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | By the 20th of each month, the Revenue Cycle Director shall produce the Monthly Payment Reconciliation Summary Report (Appendix B) for the prior month. | Revenue Cycle Director | By 20th of following month |
| 6.4.2 | The report shall include: (a) total claims paid; (b) total expected payment; (c) total actual payment; (d) total variance; (e) number of claims with variance >$100; (f) variance by category (underpayment, overpayment, adjustment); (g) open variances pending investigation; (h) overpayments referred to Compliance Officer; (i) underpayments pursued; (j) trending comparison to prior 3 months; (k) reconciliation completion rate. | Revenue Cycle Director | With report |
| 6.4.3 | CFO shall review and sign the monthly report within 5 business days. | CFO | Within 5 business days |
| 6.4.4 | Quarterly summary data shall be included in the financial report to the Governing Body per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.2. | CFO / Administrator | Quarterly |
| 6.4.5 | Reconciliation data shall be integrated into the QAPI dashboard as a revenue cycle performance indicator per QA-PI-002 and FN-FP-003. | QAPI Coordinator | Quarterly |

### 6.5 Non-Medicare Payer Reconciliation

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | For Medicare Advantage plans, reconcile payments against the contracted rate schedule per FN-FP-001. Document variances on a separate section of Appendix A. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly |
| 6.5.2 | For Medi-Cal, reconcile payments against the applicable Medi-Cal fee schedule. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly |
| 6.5.3 | For commercial payers, reconcile payments against contracted rates per FN-FP-001. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly |
| 6.5.4 | All non-Medicare payer variances exceeding contracted rate by more than 5% shall be investigated within 10 business days. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 10 business days |

### 6.6 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Payment variance >$100 identified | Revenue Cycle Director investigates per §6.3 | Within 10 business days |
| Overpayment identified | Compliance Officer notified per FN-BC-004 | Within 24 hours |
| Underpayment not resolved within 60 days | CFO notified | At 60 days |
| Reconciliation completion rate falls below 95% | CFO notified; staffing and process review | Within 5 business days |
| Systematic payment discrepancy pattern | Compliance Officer and CFO; RCA per QA-AE-002 | Within 30 calendar days |
| Total monthly variance exceeds 3% of total expected payment | CFO and Administrator notified; Governing Body informed at next meeting | Within 5 business days |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reconciliation completed for all paid claims within 30 days | Appendix A completion rate | 100% |
| Variances >$100 investigated within 10 business days | Appendix A investigation date tracking | 100% |
| Overpayments reported to Compliance Officer within 24 hours | FN-BC-004 cross-reference | 100% |
| Monthly reconciliation report completed by 20th | Report file date | 100% |
| CFO reviews monthly report within 5 business days | CFO signature date | 100% |
| Total unresolved variance <3% of expected payment | Monthly report calculation | <3% |
| Underpayments pursued within 30 days | Appendix A action tracking | 100% |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Remittance not reconciled — payments posted without verification | Overpayments retained unknowingly (60-day rule violation); underpayments undetected | §6.2 mandatory reconciliation for 100% of claims |
| Expected PDGM payment calculated incorrectly | Variances missed or misclassified | §6.2.2 standardized calculation using current CMS tables |
| Overpayment identified but not reported to Compliance Officer | 60-day clock running without tracking; reverse false claim | §6.3.4 mandatory 24-hour notification |
| Systematic underpayment pattern undetected | Ongoing revenue loss | Monthly report trending per §6.4 |
| Non-Medicare payer payments not reconciled | Contracted rate violations undetected; revenue loss | §6.5 non-Medicare reconciliation |
