# FN-CM-001 — PDGM Classification & Coding Accuracy
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-CM-001 |
| Title | PDGM Classification & Coding Accuracy |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | CM — Coding & Classification__ |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10__ |
| Status | ACTIVE__ |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual__ |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted__ |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.__ |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 484.205 (HH PPS/PDGM), 42 CFR § 484.100, CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10, ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines, 31 U.S.C. §§ 3729–3733 (False Claims Act), OIG Compliance Program Guidance |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes standards for accurate Patient-Driven Groupings Model (PDGM) classification of all Medicare home health 30-day payment periods at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. PDGM determines payment through five classification elements: admission source and timing, clinical grouping (based on the principal diagnosis), functional impairment level (based on OASIS functional items), comorbidity adjustment (based on secondary diagnoses), and episode timing (early vs. late periods). Accurate PDGM classification is essential because: (a) it determines the agency's reimbursement — incorrect classification results in either overpayment (creating 60-day rule liability) or underpayment (revenue loss); (b) it is directly tied to the accuracy of ICD-10 coding and OASIS assessment data; (c) CMS auditors, RACs, and OIG specifically target PDGM classification accuracy as a key audit area; (d) systematic misclassification constitutes False Claims Act exposure. This policy ensures every PDGM element is verified, documented, and traceable to the clinical record.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All certified coders performing diagnosis coding or PDGM classification
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (OASIS accuracy affecting functional level)
All OASIS-authorized clinicians (functional scoring)
Compliance Officer (coding audit oversight)
Billing Coordinators (pre-billing verification of PDGM)
QAPI Coordinator (coding quality integration)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Every 30-day payment period billed to Medicare by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall be classified under PDGM using accurate, documentation-supported data for all five classification elements.
4.2 PDGM classification shall be performed or verified by a certified coder (CCS, CCS-P, CPC, CPC-H, HCS-D, or equivalent nationally recognized coding credential) for every 30-day period before the final claim is submitted per FN-BC-001 §6.1.2.10.
4.3 The principal diagnosis code driving the clinical grouping shall be the primary reason for the home health episode and must be supported by physician documentation in the Plan of Care and the clinical record per FN-CM-002.
4.4 The functional impairment level shall be derived from OASIS functional assessment items completed per CL-OA policies. OASIS functional data used for PDGM classification must be accurate, contemporaneous, and unmodified for payment purposes.
4.5 The comorbidity adjustment shall be based on secondary diagnosis codes that are: (a) actively addressed during the episode; (b) documented by the physician; (c) coded to the highest specificity per ICD-10-CM guidelines; and (d) verified by a certified coder.
4.6 Admission source (institutional vs. community) and timing (early vs. late) shall be verified against the patient's actual admission history and prior claims data.
4.7 The agency shall NEVER: (a) select a principal diagnosis primarily to maximize payment rather than to reflect the primary reason for care; (b) manipulate OASIS functional scores to increase the functional impairment level for payment purposes; (c) add secondary diagnoses not supported by physician documentation to trigger a higher comorbidity adjustment; (d) misrepresent admission source or timing. Any such conduct constitutes upcoding and is prohibited per FN-BC-001 §4.6 and CO-FA-003.
4.8 Quarterly PDGM classification audits shall be conducted per §6.4 to verify accuracy across all five classification elements. Audit results shall be reported to the CFO and Compliance Officer.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| PDGM | Patient-Driven Groupings Model — CMS payment methodology classifying each 30-day payment period into one of 432 payment groups based on five elements. |
| Clinical Grouping | One of 12 clinical groups determined by the principal diagnosis ICD-10 code: Musculoskeletal Rehabilitation, Neuro/Stroke Rehabilitation, Wounds (Post-op and Trauma), Wounds (Diabetic and Vascular), Complex Nursing Interventions, Medication Management/Teaching/Assessment (MMTA) — Surgical Aftercare, MMTA — Cardiac/Circulatory, MMTA — Endocrine, MMTA — GI/GU, MMTA — Infectious Disease, MMTA — Respiratory, MMTA — Other, Behavioral Health.__ |
| Functional Impairment Level | Classification (Low, Medium, High) based on responses to specific OASIS functional assessment items that measure the patient's level of functional limitation. |
| Comorbidity Adjustment | Payment adjustment (None, Low, High) based on the presence of secondary diagnoses that are expected to increase the cost of providing home health services. |
| Admission Source | Classification as Institutional (admitted from an acute care hospital, SNF, or other institutional setting with a qualifying institutional claim within 14 days prior to SOC) or Community (all other admissions). |
| Timing | Classification as Early (first 30-day period) or Late (second or subsequent 30-day period within a certification period). |
| HIPPS Code | Health Insurance Prospective Payment System Code — the five-character code on the claim that represents the PDGM payment group. |
| Case-Mix Weight | The relative weight assigned to each PDGM payment group, reflecting the expected resource utilization for that group relative to the average.__ |
| Upcoding | Assigning codes or classification elements that result in higher payment than the clinical documentation supports. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 PDGM Classification Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Upon completion of the comprehensive assessment (SOC, ROC, or Recertification), the assessing clinician shall complete all OASIS items accurately per CL-OA policies, including all functional assessment items used in PDGM functional impairment scoring__ | Assessing Clinician | Per CL-OA-015 timeframes |
| 6.1.2 | The certified coder shall review the clinical record and assign the principal diagnosis (ICD-10-CM code) per FN-CM-002. The principal diagnosis shall reflect the primary reason the patient is receiving home health services as documented by the physician in the Plan of Care. | Certified Coder | Within 5 business days of assessment completion__ |
| 6.1.3 | The certified coder shall assign all applicable secondary/comorbidity diagnosis codes per FN-CM-002, verifying that each secondary code: (a) is documented by the physician; (b) is actively addressed during the episode; (c) is coded to the highest ICD-10-CM specificity. | Certified Coder | With principal diagnosis assignment |
| 6.1.4 | The certified coder shall verify the PDGM clinical grouping by confirming that the principal diagnosis ICD-10 code maps to the correct clinical group per the current CMS PDGM Grouper__ | Certified Coder | After diagnosis assignment |
| 6.1.5 | The certified coder or billing coordinator shall verify the functional impairment level by confirming the OASIS functional item responses produce the correct functional score per the CMS PDGM functional scoring algorithm. The coder shall NOT modify OASIS responses — if a discrepancy is identified, it shall be referred to the Director of Nursing for clinical review per CL-OA-002__ | Certified Coder + Director of Nursing (if discrepancy) | After OASIS completion__ |
| 6.1.__ | The certified coder shall verify the comorbidity adjustment by confirming that the secondary diagnoses map to the appropriate CMS comorbidity subgroups (None, Low, High) per the current CMS comorbidity interaction list__ | Certified Coder | After secondary diagnosis assignment |
| 6.1.7 | The billing coordinator shall verify the admission source by reviewing the patient's prior claims history (checking for an institutional claim within 14 days prior to SOC using the CMS claims data or agency intake records). Community vs. Institutional classification shall be documented. | Billing Coordinator | Before claim submission |
| 6.1.8 | The billing coordinator shall verify the timing (Early vs. Late) based on whether this is the first or subsequent 30-day period within the certification period. | Billing Coordinator | Before claim submission |
| 6.1.9 | The certified coder shall document the complete PDGM classification on the PDGM Classification Worksheet (Appendix A) including all five elements, the resulting HIPPS code, and the expected case-mix weight. | Certified Coder | Before claim submission |
| 6.1.10 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall verify the HIPPS code on the final claim matches the PDGM Classification Worksheet before authorizing claim submission per FN-BC-001 §6.1.2.10__ | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim submission |

### 6.2 Clinical Grouping Verification

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | The certified coder shall use the current CMS PDGM Grouper (or equivalent validated grouping tool within the EHR/billing system) to determine the clinical group based on the assigned principal diagnosis. | Certified Coder | At time of coding__ |
| 6.2.2 | If the principal diagnosis maps to an unexpected clinical group, the coder shall verify: (a) the diagnosis code is correct per clinical documentation; (b) the code is at the highest specificity; (c) the clinical group assignment is consistent with the CMS grouper logic. The coder shall NOT change the diagnosis code to achieve a different clinical group unless the original code was incorrect per the documentation__ | Certified Coder | At time of coding |
| 6.2.3 | If the coder identifies that the physician-documented primary diagnosis does not appear to represent the primary reason for home health services, the coder shall query the Director of Nursing and/or physician per FN-CM-002 §6.3 before assigning a different principal diagnosis. | Certified Coder + DO__ | Within 3 business days |

### 6.3 Functional Impairment Level Verification

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | The functional impairment level is derived from specific OASIS items scored during the comprehensive assessment. The assessing clinician is solely responsible for the accuracy of these items per CL-OA policies__ | Assessing Clinician | At time of assessment |
| 6.3.2 | The certified coder shall verify that the functional impairment level calculated by the system matches the OASIS item responses. If a discrepancy is identified (e.g., the system calculates a different level than expected based on the responses), the coder shall report the discrepancy to the Director of Nursing for review. | Certified Coder | After OASIS completion |
| 6.3.3 | Under no circumstances shall the certified coder, billing staff, or any non-clinical personnel modify OASIS functional item responses. If a clinical error is identified, correction must follow the OASIS error correction process per CL-OA-002 and be performed by the responsible clinician. | All personnel | Always |
| 6.3.4 | The Director of Nursing shall monitor OASIS functional scoring patterns across clinicians. Significant scoring variation between clinicians for similar patient populations shall trigger inter-rater reliability review per CL-OA-018__ | Director of Nursing | Quarterly |

### 6.4 Quarterly PDGM Classification Audit

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | The Compliance Officer (or designated audit lead) shall conduct a quarterly PDGM classification audit reviewing a minimum of 10% of 30-day periods billed in the prior quarter (minimum 20 claims, or all claims if fewer than 200 billed). | Compliance Officer / External Audit Consultant | Quarterly — completed within 30 calendar days of quarter end |
| 6.4.2 | The audit shall verify all five PDGM elements for each sampled claim: (a) principal diagnosis accuracy and clinical group assignment; (b) secondary diagnoses and comorbidity adjustment; (c) OASIS functional items and functional level; (d) admission source; (e) timing. | Audit Lead | During audit |
| 6.4.3 | For each audited claim, the auditor shall independently determine the correct PDGM classification based on the clinical record and compare it to the classification billed. Any discrepancy is a finding. | Audit Lead | During audit |
| 6.4.4 | Audit results shall be documented in the Quarterly PDGM Audit Report (Appendix B) including: (a) sample size; (b) accuracy rate for each PDGM element; (c) overall classification accuracy rate; (d) findings detail; (e) financial impact of errors (overpayments and underpayments); (f) root cause analysis for any error rate >5%. | Audit Lead | Within 30 calendar days of audit completion |
| 6.4.5 | The audit report shall be reviewed by the Revenue Cycle Director, CFO, Compliance Officer, and Director of Nursing within 14 calendar days. | All named | Within 14 calendar days |
| 6.4.6 | Any overpayment identified through the audit shall be referred to the Compliance Officer for processing per FN-BC-004 within 24 hours. | Compliance Officer | Within 24 hours |
| 6.4.7 | If the overall PDGM classification accuracy rate falls below 95%, a corrective action plan shall be developed per QA-AE-003 within 30 calendar days. | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer | Within 30 calendar days |
| 6.4.8 | Audit results shall be reported to the QAPI Committee per QA-PI-002 and the Governing Body in the quarterly compliance report. | Compliance Officer | Quarterly |

### 6.5 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Coder uncertain about clinical grouping | Query DON and/or physician per §6.2.3 | Within 3 business days |
| OASIS functional score discrepancy identified | DON review per §6.3.2 | Within 5 business days |
| Suspected upcoding pattern | Compliance Officer per CO-CP-007 | Within 24 hours |
| Quarterly audit accuracy <95% | CFO and Compliance Officer; CAP per QA-AE-003 | Within 30 calendar days |
| Quarterly audit identifies overpayments | Compliance Officer per FN-BC-004 | Within 24 hours |
| Individual coder accuracy <90__ | Revenue Cycle Director; targeted training and supervision | Within 14 calendar days |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PDGM Classification Worksheet completed for all billed periods | Appendix A audit | 100% |
| Certified coder verifies all PDGM elements before claim submission | Pre-billing verification per FN-BC-001 | 100% |
| Quarterly PDGM audit completed | Appendix B on file | 100% (4 audits per year)__ |
| Overall PDGM classification accuracy | Quarterly audit results | ≥ 95% |
| Clinical grouping accuracy | Quarterly audit results | ≥ 95% |
| Functional level accuracy | Quarterly audit results | ≥ 95% |
| Comorbidity adjustment accuracy | Quarterly audit results | ≥ 95% |
| Overpayments from classification errors reported per FN-BC-004 | Cross-reference to overpayment log | 100% within 24 hours |
| Zero upcoding findings | Audit and compliance investigation records | Zero |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Principal diagnosis selected for payment rather than clinical reason | Upcoding; False Claims Act; PDGM misclassification | §4.7 prohibition; §6.2 clinical grouping verification; quarterly audit |
| OASIS functional scores inflated | Higher functional level = higher payment; OIG audit target | §6.3.3 prohibition on non-clinical modification; CL-OA-018 inter-rater reliability |
| Secondary diagnoses added without physician documentation | Comorbidity adjustment inflation; upcoding | §4.5 documentation requirement; FN-CM-002; quarterly audit |
| Admission source misidentified | Institutional vs. Community misclassification | §6.1.7 claims history verification |
| No PDGM verification before billing | Classification errors pass through to claims | FN-BC-001 pre-billing verification; Appendix A mandatory |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All certified coders shall receive initial training on PDGM classification within 14 calendar days of hire. Training shall cover: (a) PDGM payment methodology and all five classification elements; (b) CMS PDGM grouper logic; (c) clinical grouping determination from ICD-10 codes; (d) functional impairment scoring from OASIS items; (e) comorbidity adjustment determination; (f) admission source verification; (g) timing determination; (h) PDGM Classification Worksheet completion; (i) prohibition on upcoding and coding for payment; (j) False Claims Act liability for coding errors.
10.2 Annual refresher training including CMS PDGM updates, rate changes, and grouper changes effective each calendar year.
10.3 All in-scope personnel shall sign Appendix C within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require CFO and Compliance Officer review, Governing Body approval, and re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.

# FN-CM-002 — ICD-10 Coding Standards & Accuracy
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-CM-002 |
| Title | ICD-10 Coding Standards & Accuracy |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | CM — Coding & Classification |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 484.100, ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines for Coding and Reporting (NCHS/CMS), HIPAA Code Set Standards (45 CFR § 162.1002), 31 U.S.C. §§ 3729–3733 (False Claims Act), OIG Compliance Program Guidance |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes comprehensive standards for ICD-10-CM diagnosis coding at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to ensure that all diagnosis codes assigned to home health episodes are accurate, supported by physician documentation in the medical record, coded to the highest specificity per the ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines, and compliant with all applicable federal coding regulations. Accurate diagnosis coding is critical because: (a) the principal diagnosis determines the PDGM clinical grouping and directly affects payment per FN-CM-001; (b) secondary diagnoses determine comorbidity adjustments; (c) inaccurate coding constitutes potential False Claims Act liability; (d) coding accuracy is a primary OIG and RAC audit target for home health agencies; (e) diagnosis codes drive clinical quality measures reported through OASIS.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All certified coders performing diagnosis coding
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (physician documentation adequacy)
All OASIS-authorized clinicians (diagnosis selection during assessment)
Compliance Officer (coding audit oversight)
All physicians ordering home health services (documentation of diagnoses)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 All diagnosis codes assigned to home health episodes at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall comply with the current ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines for Coding and Reporting as published by NCHS and CMS, and the HIPAA code set standards (45 CFR § 162.1002).
4.2 Every diagnosis code — principal and secondary — must be supported by physician documentation in the medical record. Coders shall not assign diagnosis codes based solely on clinical assessment by non-physician clinicians unless the physician has acknowledged and documented the diagnosis.
4.3 The principal diagnosis shall represent the primary reason the patient is receiving home health services. Selection of the principal diagnosis shall follow the CMS OASIS Guidance Manual and ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines for sequencing in home health.
4.4 All diagnosis codes shall be assigned to the highest level of specificity available in the current ICD-10-CM code set. Unspecified codes shall be used only when clinical documentation does not provide sufficient detail for a more specific code, and a physician query has been attempted.
4.5 Only certified coders (holding CCS, CCS-P, CPC, CPC-H, HCS-D, or equivalent nationally recognized credential) shall perform final diagnosis code assignment and verification for billing purposes. Clinicians may select preliminary diagnosis codes during OASIS completion, but final coding for billing shall be verified by a certified coder.
4.6 Coders shall query the physician when documentation is ambiguous, incomplete, or insufficient to assign an accurate code. Queries shall be documented in the coding record. Coders shall not assign codes based on assumptions or clinical inference not documented by the physician.
4.7 Quarterly coding accuracy audits shall be conducted per §6.4 with a minimum accuracy standard of 95%.
4.8 The agency shall NEVER: (a) assign diagnosis codes not documented by the physician; (b) assign codes to maximize reimbursement rather than reflect the documented clinical picture; (c) use unspecified codes when more specific codes are available and documentation supports them; (d) assign resolved or inactive diagnoses as current conditions; (e) change physician-documented diagnoses without physician authorization. Any such conduct constitutes coding fraud.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| ICD-10-CM | International Classification of Diseases, Tenth Revision, Clinical Modification — the standard diagnosis code set required for Medicare claims per HIPAA. |
| Principal Diagnosis | The diagnosis most related to the current plan of care — the condition that is the primary reason the patient is receiving home health services. In home health, this is the diagnosis that drives the clinical grouping under PDGM. |
| Secondary Diagnosis | All conditions that coexist at the time of the assessment, that are actively addressed during the episode, or that affect patient care or treatment. |
| Highest Specificity | Assignment of the ICD-10-CM code with the greatest number of characters available that accurately reflects the documented diagnosis. A 3-character category code shall not be used when 4, 5, 6, or 7-character codes are available. |
| Physician Query | A formal communication to the physician requesting clarification, additional documentation, or confirmation of a diagnosis when the existing documentation is insufficient for accurate code assignment. |
| Coding Guideline | A rule or convention from the ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines for Coding and Reporting that governs how codes are to be assigned and sequenced. |
| Certified Coder | A coding professional holding a nationally recognized coding credential (CCS, CCS-P, CPC, CPC-H, HCS-D, or equivalent) who has demonstrated competency in ICD-10-CM coding. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Diagnosis Code Assignment Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | The assessing clinician selects preliminary diagnosis codes during the comprehensive assessment (SOC/ROC/Recertification) based on the physician's Plan of Care and clinical documentation. | Assessing Clinician | At time of assessment |
| 6.1.2 | The certified coder reviews the complete clinical record — including the Plan of Care, physician orders, hospital discharge summary (if applicable), progress notes, and OASIS — to verify and finalize all diagnosis codes. | Certified Coder | Within 5 business days of assessment completion |
| 6.1.3 | The coder shall verify that the principal diagnosis: (a) represents the primary reason for home health services; (b) is documented by the physician on the Plan of Care; (c) is coded to the highest ICD-10-CM specificity; (d) follows CMS OASIS Guidance Manual sequencing rules; (e) maps to the appropriate PDGM clinical grouping per FN-CM-001. | Certified Coder | During code assignment |
| 6.1.4 | The coder shall verify that all secondary diagnoses: (a) are documented by the physician; (b) are actively addressed during the episode or affect care; (c) are coded to the highest specificity; (d) are not resolved/historical conditions coded as current; (e) correctly trigger the PDGM comorbidity adjustment per FN-CM-001. | Certified Coder | During code assignment |
| 6.1.5 | The coder shall document the final code selection on the Diagnosis Coding Worksheet (Appendix A) with each code, its description, the documentation source supporting the code, and the specificity level. | Certified Coder | At time of finalization |

### 6.2 Specificity Requirements

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | For every diagnosis code, the coder shall assign the code to the maximum number of characters available in ICD-10-CM that the documentation supports. | Certified Coder | During code assignment |
| 6.2.2 | If the documentation supports only an unspecified code (e.g., laterality not documented, type not specified), the coder shall: (a) first attempt to obtain the missing detail from the clinical record; (b) if not found, submit a physician query per §6.3; (c) only use the unspecified code if the query does not yield additional information. Document the query attempt. | Certified Coder | During code assignment |
| 6.2.3 | The coder shall apply all applicable ICD-10-CM conventions including: 7th character requirements for injuries and musculoskeletal conditions; combination codes; sequencing rules for etiology/manifestation; external cause codes (where applicable); Z-codes for aftercare and encounters. | Certified Coder | During code assignment |

### 6.3 Physician Query Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | When physician documentation is ambiguous, incomplete, conflicting, or insufficient to assign an accurate ICD-10-CM code, the certified coder shall initiate a physician query. | Certified Coder | When deficiency identified |
| 6.3.2 | The query shall be documented using the Physician Query Form (Appendix B) and shall: (a) reference the specific clinical documentation in question; (b) explain the coding issue requiring clarification; (c) NOT lead the physician toward a specific diagnosis or suggest a particular code; (d) present clinical options when applicable (e.g., "Is this condition best described as X or Y per your clinical assessment?"); (e) request the physician's documented response within 7 calendar days. | Certified Coder | At time of query |
| 6.3.3 | The query shall be transmitted to the physician via the EHR message system, fax, or secure email. The Director of Nursing shall facilitate physician communication if the coder does not have direct physician access. | Certified Coder / Director of Nursing | Same day as query initiation |
| 6.3.4 | If the physician responds within 7 calendar days, the coder shall assign the code based on the physician's clarification and document the response in the coding record. | Certified Coder | Upon response receipt |
| 6.3.5 | If the physician does not respond within 7 calendar days, the coder shall: (a) send a second query attempt; (b) notify the Director of Nursing for escalation; (c) if still no response by day 14, assign the code based on the best available documentation and document "Physician query unanswered — code assigned per available documentation." | Certified Coder / Director of Nursing | Day 7 (second attempt); Day 14 (code based on documentation) |
| 6.3.6 | All queries and responses shall be retained in the coding record as part of the audit trail per CO-DC-001. | Certified Coder | Ongoing |

### 6.4 Quarterly Coding Accuracy Audit

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | The Compliance Officer shall conduct a quarterly ICD-10 coding accuracy audit reviewing a minimum of 10% of episodes coded in the prior quarter (minimum 20 episodes). | Compliance Officer / External Audit | Quarterly |
| 6.4.2 | The audit shall evaluate: (a) principal diagnosis accuracy; (b) principal diagnosis specificity; (c) secondary diagnosis accuracy and completeness; (d) secondary diagnosis specificity; (e) sequencing compliance with ICD-10-CM guidelines and OASIS guidance; (f) physician documentation support for every assigned code. | Audit Lead | During audit |
| 6.4.3 | Results shall be documented in the Quarterly Coding Accuracy Audit Report (Appendix C). | Audit Lead | Within 30 calendar days |
| 6.4.4 | Overall coding accuracy standard: ≥95%. Individual coder accuracy standard: ≥90%. Failure to meet standards triggers corrective action. | Compliance Officer / Revenue Cycle Director | Per audit results |
| 6.4.5 | Audit results reported to CFO, Compliance Officer, DON, QAPI Committee, and Governing Body quarterly. | Compliance Officer | Quarterly |

### 6.5 Annual ICD-10-CM Update Process

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Each October 1, CMS publishes ICD-10-CM code updates. The Revenue Cycle Director shall ensure all coders receive training on new, revised, and deleted codes before the effective date. | Revenue Cycle Director | Training completed by September 30 each year |
| 6.5.2 | The billing and EHR systems shall be updated with the current ICD-10-CM code set before October 1. | IT Director / Revenue Cycle Director | By September 30 |
| 6.5.3 | The certified coder shall verify that no deleted or revised codes are used on claims submitted on or after October 1. | Certified Coder | Ongoing from October 1 |

### 6.6 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Physician documentation insufficient for accurate coding | Physician query per §6.3 | Within 3 business days |
| Physician query unanswered after 14 days | DON escalation to physician/practice | Within 14 calendar days |
| Suspected upcoding or code manipulation | Compliance Officer per CO-CP-007 | Within 24 hours |
| Quarterly audit accuracy <95% | CFO and Compliance Officer; CAP per QA-AE-003 | Within 30 calendar days |
| Individual coder accuracy <90% | Revenue Cycle Director; supervised re-training | Within 14 calendar days |
| ICD-10-CM annual update not implemented by October 1 | IT Director and Revenue Cycle Director; emergency system update | Immediately |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Retention |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Code assignment | Diagnosis Coding Worksheet (Appendix A) | Certified Coder | Coding file / billing system | 7 years |
| Physician queries | Physician Query Form (Appendix B) with response | Certified Coder | Coding file / clinical record | 7 years |
| Quarterly audit | Quarterly Coding Accuracy Audit Report (Appendix C) | Compliance Officer | Compliance/QAPI file | 7 years |
| Coder credentials | Current coding certification documentation | HR Director | Personnel files | Duration of employment + 7 years |
| Annual update training | Training completion records for ICD-10 annual updates | Revenue Cycle Director | Training records | 7 years |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix D | Revenue Cycle Director | Policy acknowledgment file | 7 years |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All codes assigned by certified coders | Personnel file / credential verification | 100% |
| Quarterly coding audit completed | Appendix C on file | 100% (4 per year) |
| Overall coding accuracy | Quarterly audit | ≥ 95% |
| Individual coder accuracy | Quarterly audit by coder | ≥ 90% |
| All codes supported by physician documentation | Audit finding rate | Zero unsupported codes |
| Codes assigned to highest specificity | Audit specificity rate | ≥ 95% |
| Physician queries documented | Query log review | 100% of queries documented |
| Annual ICD-10 update training completed | Training records | 100% by September 30 |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Code assigned without physician documentation | Upcoding; FCA exposure | §4.2 documentation requirement; quarterly audit |
| Unspecified code used when specificity available | Audit finding; revenue impact | §6.2 specificity requirements; physician query |
| Clinician assigns final codes without coder verification | Non-certified coding; accuracy risk | §4.5 certified coder requirement |
| Physician documentation uses vague/ambiguous terminology | Coding accuracy impaired | §6.3 physician query process |
| ICD-10 annual updates not reflected in system | Rejected claims; coding errors | §6.5 annual update process |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All certified coders shall receive initial training within 14 calendar days covering: ICD-10-CM Official Guidelines; home health-specific coding rules; OASIS diagnosis sequencing; specificity requirements; physician query process; coding ethics and prohibited practices; FCA implications.
10.2 Annual ICD-10-CM update training by September 30 each year.
10.3 Annual coding refresher with competency assessment (≥80% pass rate).
10.4 All in-scope personnel shall sign Appendix D within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require CFO and Compliance Officer review, Governing Body approval, and re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days.

# FN-CM-003 — Medical Necessity Documentation
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-CM-003 |
| Title | Medical Necessity Documentation |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | CM — Coding & Classification |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual__ |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 409.42 (Covered Home Health Services — Reasonable and Necessary), 42 CFR § 424.22, 42 CFR § 484.55, CMS IOM Pub. 100-02 Ch. 7, CMS LCD/NCD Requirements, 31 U.S.C. §§ 3729–3733 |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes documentation standards to ensure that the medical necessity of all home health services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. is clearly, completely, and contemporaneously documented in the clinical record to support Medicare billing. Under 42 CFR § 409.42, Medicare covers home health services only when they are reasonable and necessary for the diagnosis and treatment of illness or injury or to improve the functioning of a malformed body member. Every service billed to Medicare must be supported by documentation that demonstrates: (a) the patient has a qualifying condition requiring skilled care; (b) the specific service is reasonable and necessary for the condition; (c) the patient meets homebound criteria; (d) the service is provided under a physician-ordered Plan of Care; (e) the expected outcome cannot be achieved by the patient or caregiver without skilled intervention. Insufficient medical necessity documentation is the single most common reason for Medicare home health claim denials and is the primary focus of RAC, CERT, and OIG audits.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager (clinical documentation oversight)
All clinical staff providing direct patient care (documentation of medical necessity in visit notes)
All OASIS-authorized clinicians
All certified coders (coding alignment with documented necessity)
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director (billing alignment)
Compliance Officer (audit oversight)
All physicians ordering home health services
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Every visit note, assessment, and Plan of Care shall document the medical necessity of the services provided with sufficient specificity to demonstrate to an auditor or reviewer why the service was required for this specific patient at this specific time.
4.2 Medical necessity documentation shall address all four Medicare coverage criteria for each episode: (a) the patient is under the care of a physician who has established a Plan of Care; (b) the patient needs skilled nursing, physical therapy, speech-language pathology, or continued occupational therapy services; (c) the patient is homebound; (d) the services are reasonable and necessary for the treatment of the patient's condition.
4.3 Documentation of skilled care need shall articulate: (a) the specific skilled service(s) required; (b) the clinical rationale for why the service requires a skilled clinician (as opposed to a non-skilled caregiver); (c) the patient-specific treatment goals; (d) the expected duration and frequency of services; (e) the patient's progress toward goals or documented clinical justification for continued services when progress is limited.
4.4 Homebound status shall be documented with patient-specific narrative (not generic/templated language) in every visit note per CL-CA-005. The narrative shall describe the specific condition(s) that restrict the patient from leaving home and the taxing effort or assistance required.
4.5 For patients with chronic, stable conditions, documentation shall clearly articulate why the patient continues to require skilled services and what specific skilled interventions are being provided that a non-skilled caregiver could not perform.
4.6 The Director of Nursing shall conduct targeted medical necessity documentation reviews monthly per §6.3 to identify documentation deficiencies before claims are submitted.
4.7 All clinical staff shall receive initial and annual training on medical necessity documentation standards per §10.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Medical Necessity | The requirement under 42 CFR § 409.42 that covered services must be reasonable and necessary for the diagnosis and treatment of illness or injury or to improve the functioning of a malformed body member. Services are considered reasonable and necessary when they require the skills of a qualified clinician, are provided at a level of complexity inherent to the condition, and are consistent with accepted standards of clinical practice. |
| Skilled Service | A service that requires the skills of a licensed nurse or therapist to be performed safely and effectively. A service is not skilled if it could be safely and effectively performed by a non-medical person without supervision. |
| Homebound Status | A patient is considered homebound if: (1) the condition, due to illness or injury, restricts their ability to leave the home; and (2) leaving home requires a considerable and taxing effort. A patient may leave home for medical treatment, religious services, or infrequent/short absences for non-medical reasons without losing homebound status. |
| Reasonable and Necessary | Services that are: (a) clinically appropriate in terms of type, frequency, and duration; (b) provided in accordance with accepted standards of practice; (c) furnished at a level of complexity inherent to the patient's condition; (d) not primarily for the convenience of the patient or provider.__ |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Medical Necessity Documentation Requirements by Visit Type

| Visit Type | Required Medical Necessity Documentation Elements | Responsible Party |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SOC/ROC Assessment | (a) Qualifying diagnosis requiring skilled services; (b) specific skilled services needed with clinical rationale; (c) patient-specific homebound status narrative; (d) baseline functional status; (e) measurable treatment goals with timeframes; (f) expected outcomes; (g) patient/caregiver education needs; (h) frequency and duration of services ordered with rationale | Assessing Clinician |
| Skilled Nursing Visit | (a) Reason for today's visit tied to Plan of Care goals; (b) skilled assessment findings; (c) skilled interventions performed; (d) patient response to interventions; (e) progress toward goals; (f) continued homebound status narrative; (g) continued need for skilled services; (h) plan for next visit | RN/LVN |
| Therapy Visit (PT/OT/SLP) | (a) Objective measurements (ROM, strength, balance, functional mobility, swallow function, cognition as applicable); (b) skilled therapeutic interventions performed; (c) patient performance and response; (d) progress toward functional goals (quantified); (e) continued homebound status; (f) clinical rationale for continued therapy; (g) rehabilitation potential assessment | Therapist |
| HHA Supervisory Visit | (a) Aide services provided align with Plan of Care; (b) patient response to aide services; (c) assessment of continued need for aide services; (d) aide competency observation | RN |
| Recertification Assessment | All SOC elements PLUS: (a) justification for continued services; (b) documentation of progress or clinical rationale for maintenance if limited progress; (c) reassessment of goals with updated targets; (d) updated homebound status | Assessing Clinician |
| Discharge | (a) Summary of services provided; (b) goals met/not met with explanation; (c) patient status at discharge; (d) reason for discharge; (e) discharge instructions | Discharging Clinician |

### 6.2 Prohibited Documentation Practices

| Prohibited Practice | Explanation |
| --- | --- |
| Generic homebound language | Phrases such as "patient is homebound" without patient-specific description of restricting condition(s) are insufficient and will not survive audit. |
| Templated/copied-forward visit notes without individualization | Visit notes that are identical or near-identical across visits suggest lack of skilled assessment. Each note must reflect that specific encounter. |
| Documentation of "monitoring" or "assessment" without skilled intervention | Mere monitoring of a stable condition does not meet skilled care requirements. Document what skilled action was taken and why. |
| Documentation that suggests custodial care | Language implying services are for personal care, convenience, or general supervision without skilled need. |
| Failure to address limited progress | If a patient is not progressing toward goals, clinical reasoning for continued services must be documented (e.g., maintenance therapy under Jimmo v. Sebelius, prevention of decline, complexity requiring skilled monitoring). |
| Backdated or fabricated documentation | Creating documentation after the fact to support billing is prohibited per CO-DC-003 and constitutes fraud. |

### 6.3 Monthly Medical Necessity Documentation Review

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | The Director of Nursing shall review a minimum of 5% of visit notes monthly (minimum 10 notes) for medical necessity documentation compliance. | Director of Nursing | Monthly |
| 6.3.2 | The review shall evaluate: (a) skilled service documented with clinical rationale; (b) homebound status narrative present and patient-specific; (c) progress toward goals documented (or clinical reasoning for limited progress); (d) visit purpose tied to Plan of Care; (e) no prohibited documentation practices. | Director of Nursing | During review |
| 6.3.3 | Results shall be documented on the Monthly Medical Necessity Review Log (Appendix A). | Director of Nursing | Monthly |
| 6.3.__ | Clinicians with documentation deficiency rates >10% shall receive targeted re-training within 14 calendar days. | Director of Nursing | Within 14 calendar days |
| 6.3.5 | Review results shall be reported to the Revenue Cycle Director (billing impact), Compliance Officer (compliance correlation), and QAPI Coordinator (quality integration). | Director of Nursing | Monthly__ |

### 6.4 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Visit note lacks medical necessity documentation | DON returns note to clinician for correction per CO-DC-003 | Within 3 business days |
| Pattern of deficient documentation by specific clinician | DON provides targeted training; if unresolved after 30 days, HR-ER-002 | Within 14 calendar days |
| Medical necessity documentation deficiency identified before billing | Claim placed on billing hold per FN-BC-001 §6.1.3 | Until resolved__ |
| Medical necessity deficiency identified after billing (audit) | Compliance Officer evaluates for overpayment per FN-BC-004 | Within 24 hours |
| Systematic documentation failure across multiple clinicians | DON and Compliance Officer; agency-wide training; QAPI integration | Within 30 calendar days |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Retention |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Monthly review | Monthly Medical Necessity Review Log (Appendix A) | Director of Nursing | Clinical/compliance file | 7 years |
| Clinician re-training | Training documentation for deficient clinicians | Director of Nursing / HR | Personnel/training files | 7 years |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix B | Director of Nursing | Policy acknowledgment file | 7 years |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Monthly documentation review completed | Appendix A on file | 100% (12 per year) |
| Medical necessity documentation compliance rate | Monthly review results | ≥ 95% |
| Homebound status narrative present and patient-specific | Monthly review | ≥ 95% |
| Clinician deficiency rate ≤10% | Monthly review by clinician | 100% of clinicians at ≤10% |
| Re-training completed within 14 days for deficient clinicians | Training records | 100% |
| Zero claims denied for medical necessity documentation | Denial tracking per FN-BC-002 | Zero (target) |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generic homebound language | #1 denial reason nationally; audit vulnerability | §6.2 prohibition; CL-CA-005; monthly review |
| Visit notes do not describe skilled intervention | Denial for lack of skilled need | §6.1 required elements; monthly review |
| Therapy notes lack objective measurements | Denial for no evidence of progress | §6.1 therapy visit requirements |
| Continued services documentation inadequate at recertification | Denial for lack of continued need | §6.1 recertification requirements |
| Documentation of "monitoring" without skilled action | Custodial care finding; denial | §6.2 prohibited practice |

# FN-CM-004 — Episode Management & Authorization
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-CM-004 |
| Title | Episode Management & Authorization |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | CM — Coding & Classification |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 424.22 (Certification/Recertification), 42 CFR § 484.60, 42 CFR § 484.205, CMS IOM Pub. 100-02 Ch. 7, CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10 |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes standards for managing home health episodes including certification period tracking, recertification timing, episode transitions (SOC, ROC, transfers, discharges), and payer authorization requirements at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Proper episode management is essential to ensure: (a) physician certification and recertification are obtained within CMS-required timeframes; (b) 30-day payment periods are correctly identified and billed; (c) episode transitions (SOC, ROC, transfer, discharge) are properly documented and trigger the correct billing actions; (d) prior authorization requirements for non-Medicare payers are met; (e) no billing occurs for periods without valid certification. Failure to manage episodes properly results in denied claims, lost revenue, compliance deficiencies, and potential False Claims Act liability.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All billing coordinators
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All case managers and clinical coordinators
Intake coordinators
Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Every Medicare home health episode shall have a valid physician certification before any claims are submitted for the certification period per 42 CFR § 424.22.
4.2 Initial certification requires: (a) physician's order for home health services; (b) face-to-face encounter within the required timeframe per CL-CA-006; (c) signed Plan of Care with certification statement.
4.3 Recertification is required at least every 60 days for continued Medicare home health eligibility. The recertification process must be completed before the end of the current certification period per CL-CP-008.
4.4 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain an Episode Management Dashboard (Appendix A) tracking all open episodes with: SOC date, current certification period, recertification due date, 30-day period dates, physician signature status, and billing status.
4.5 For non-Medicare payers (Medicare Advantage, commercial), the agency shall obtain and maintain current prior authorizations per payer-specific requirements. No services shall be provided beyond authorized dates or visit counts without prior authorization renewal.
4.6 Episode transitions (ROC, transfer, discharge) shall trigger specific billing and clinical documentation actions documented in §6.
4.7 The agency shall not continue billing for episodes where physician recertification has lapsed without documented evidence that recertification was timely obtained. Lapsed certifications shall be treated as billing holds per FN-BC-001 §6.1.3.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Certification Period | A 60-day period during which Medicare home health services are authorized by physician certification. The initial certification begins with the SOC date. |
| Recertification | Physician attestation that the patient continues to meet Medicare home health eligibility criteria, obtained at or before the end of each 60-day certification period. |
| 30-Day Payment Period | The unit of PDGM payment. Each 60-day certification period contains two 30-day payment periods, each independently classified and reimbursed. |
| SO__ | Start of Care — the first day of a new home health episode. |
| ROC | Resumption of Care — the first day of resumed services after a qualifying inpatient facility stay. |
| Transfer | Movement of a patient from one home health agency to another. Triggers specific billing actions (PEP adjustment for transferring agency; new NOA for receiving agency). |
| Discharge | Termination of home health services. Triggers discharge assessment, discharge claim, and OASIS discharge. |
| Prior Authorization | Advance approval from a payer (typically non-Medicare) required before services are provided or continued, specifying authorized services, visit counts, and date ranges. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 New Episode Initiation (SOC)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Upon patient admission per OP-IM-001/OP-IM-002, initiate the episode in the billing system with: patient demographics, payer information, SOC date, attending physician, and initial certification period dates__ | Billing Coordinator / Intake | Day of admission |
| 6.1.2 | Verify: (a) physician order for home health on file; (b) F2F encounter documentation on file or tracked per CL-CA-007; (c) initial Plan of Care development in progress per CL-CP-001. | Billing Coordinator / DON | Within 5 calendar days of SOC |
| 6.1.3 | Submit NOA per FN-BC-006 within 5 calendar days. | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 days |
| 6.1.4 | Enter the episode on the Episode Management Dashboard (Appendix A) with all tracking dates__ | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 5 calendar days |
| 6.1.5 | For non-Medicare payers: obtain prior authorization before or within the payer-specified timeframe. Document authorization number, authorized services, visit counts, and date range__ | Billing Coordinator | Per payer requirements |

### 6.2 Certification Period and Recertification Tracking

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Each 60-day certification period and its two 30-day payment periods shall be tracked on Appendix A. | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing |
| 6.2.2 | At day 45 of each certification period, generate a recertification alert. Notify the Director of Nursing that the recertification assessment and physician recertification must be completed before day 60. | Revenue Cycle Director | Day 45 |
| 6.2.3 | The Director of Nursing shall ensure the recertification assessment is completed per CL-CA-004 and the physician is contacted for recertification per CL-CP-008. | Director of Nursing | Before day 60 |
| 6.2.4 | The signed recertification (physician signature on the updated Plan of Care) must be obtained and on file before any claims are submitted for the new certification period. Track per CL-CP-009. | Revenue Cycle Director | Before claim submission |
| 6.2.5 | If recertification is not obtained by day 60: (a) place all claims for the new period on billing hold; (b) notify the Director of Nursing for urgent physician follow-up; (c) if not obtained by day 75, escalate to Administrator. | Revenue Cycle Director | Day 60 (hold); Day 75 (escalation) |

### 6.3 Episode Transitions
#### 6.3.1 Resumption of Care (ROC)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | When a patient returns from an inpatient facility stay (hospital, SNF), the clinical team shall determine if a ROC assessment is required per CMS OASIS guidance__ | Director of Nursing | Upon notification of patient return |
| b | If ROC is required, complete the ROC assessment within 2 calendar days of the resumption visit per CL-CA-001. | Assessing Clinician | Within 2 days |
| c | Submit new NOA per FN-BC-006 within 5 days of the ROC date. | Billing Coordinator | Within 5 days |
| d | Update the Episode Management Dashboard with ROC date and new period dates. | Revenue Cycle Director | Same day |

#### 6.3.2 Transfer to Another HHA

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | When a patient is transferred to another HHA, complete a discharge assessment (Transfer OASIS) per CL-CP-007. | Director of Nursing | Within 2 days of last visit |
| b | Submit a discharge/transfer claim for the partial 30-day period. PEP adjustment will be applied by CMS. | Billing Coordinator | Per FN-BC-001 |
| c | Update Episode Management Dashboard with transfer date and close episode. | Revenue Cycle Director | Same day |

#### 6.3.3 Discharge

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | When a patient is discharged per CL-CP-006, complete the discharge OASIS within 2 calendar days of the last visit. | Assessing Clinician | Within 2 days |
| b | Transmit discharge OASIS to CMS. | Clinical Coordinator | Per CL-CA-003 |
| c | Submit final claim for the last 30-day period per FN-BC-001. | Billing Coordinator | Per FN-BC-001 |
| d | Issue NOMNC per FN-BC-003 §6.3 at least 2 days before last visit. | Director of Nursing | ≥2 days before last visit |
| e | Close episode on Dashboard. | Revenue Cycle Director | Same day |

### 6.4 Prior Authorization Management (Non-Medicare)

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | Maintain a Prior Authorization Tracking Log (Appendix B) for all non-Medicare episodes. | Billing Coordinator | Ongoing |
| 6.4.2 | At 14 calendar days before authorization expiration, generate a reauthorization alert and initiate the renewal process with the payer. | Billing Coordinator | 14 days before expiration |
| 6.4.3 | If reauthorization is denied or delayed: (a) notify the Director of Nursing to evaluate clinical options; (b) notify the patient per FN-BC-003; (c) issue ABN if applicable; (d) place claims on billing hold for unauthorized periods. | Billing Coordinator + DON | Within 2 business days |
| 6.4.4 | No services shall be billed for dates beyond the authorized period unless retroactive authorization is obtained. | Revenue Cycle Director | Ongoing |

### 6.5 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Certification period expiring in 15 days without recertification | DON prioritizes physician contact | Day 45 alert |
| Certification lapsed (day 60+) | Billing hold; DON urgent physician outreach; if day 75+ escalate to Administrator | Immediately |
| Prior authorization expiring in 7 days without renewal | Billing Coordinator escalates to Revenue Cycle Director | 7 days before expiration |
| Prior authorization denied | DON clinical review; patient notification; appeal if applicable | Within 2 business days |
| ROC not identified timely | DON reviews all inpatient notifications daily | Daily |

| Episode tracking | Episode Management Dashboard (Appendix A) | Revenue Cycle Director | Billing system | 7 years |
| Authorization tracking | Prior Authorization Tracking Log (Appendix B) | Billing Coordinator | Billing system | 7 years |
| Certification documentation | Signed Plan of Care / certification for each period | Director of Nursing | Clinical record | 7 years |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix C | Revenue Cycle Director | Policy acknowledgment file | 7 years |

| 100% of episodes have valid certification before billing | Appendix A cross-check with claims | 100% |
| Recertification obtained before day 60 | Appendix A tracking | ≥ 95% |
| NOA submitted within 5 days for all SOC/ROC | FN-BC-006 tracking | 100% |
| Prior authorizations current for all non-Medicare episodes | Appendix B audit | 100% |
| Zero claims submitted for periods without valid certification | Claims audit | Zero |

# FN-CM-005 — LUPA Prevention & Monitoring
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-CM-005 |
| Title | LUPA Prevention & Monitoring |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | CM — Coding & Classification |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 484.205, 42 CFR § 484.60, CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10, CMS PDGM Final Rules |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's program for monitoring visit utilization within each 30-day payment period to identify episodes at risk of Low Utilization Payment Adjustment (LUPA) and to ensure that all physician-ordered visits are delivered in accordance with the Plan of Care. Under PDGM, when the number of visits in a 30-day period falls below the CMS-established LUPA threshold for the assigned payment group, the agency receives a significantly reduced per-visit payment instead of the full PDGM case-mix adjusted payment. LUPA episodes typically reimburse at approximately 15–30% of the full payment amount, resulting in substantial revenue loss. While the agency must never add visits solely to avoid LUPA (which would constitute fraud), the agency has a clinical and operational obligation to ensure that all physician-ordered visits are actually delivered as ordered. This policy integrates clinical visit compliance (CL-SD-025), scheduling operations (OP-SL-001), and revenue cycle monitoring (FN-FP-003) into a unified LUPA prevention program managed through QAPI (QA-PI-006).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All clinical scheduling coordinators
All billing coordinators
QAPI Coordinator
Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall monitor visit utilization for every open 30-day payment period against the applicable PDGM LUPA threshold.
4.2 The LUPA threshold for each 30-day period is determined by the PDGM payment group assigned to that period. The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain the current CMS LUPA threshold table and update it annually when CMS publishes updated thresholds.
4.3 Episodes identified as at risk of LUPA (projected visits below threshold) shall be reviewed by the Director of Nursing within 48 hours to determine: (a) whether all physician-ordered visits have been scheduled and delivered; (b) whether any missed, cancelled, or rescheduled visits have reduced the count below threshold; (c) whether the patient's clinical condition supports the ordered visit frequency.
4.4 The agency shall NEVER add visits that are not clinically necessary or not physician-ordered solely to exceed the LUPA threshold. Adding visits solely for payment purposes constitutes fraud. Any LUPA prevention intervention must be grounded in the physician-ordered Plan of Care and clinical need.
4.5 Legitimate LUPA prevention actions include: (a) ensuring all physician-ordered visits are scheduled and delivered; (b) resolving scheduling barriers (patient cancellations, staff availability); (c) communicating with the physician regarding appropriate visit frequency; (d) identifying missed visits and rescheduling per CL-SD-024.
4.6 LUPA rate shall be monitored as a Key Performance Indicator (KPI) and reported monthly to the CFO, quarterly to the QAPI Committee, and quarterly to the Governing Body.
4.7 A LUPA rate exceeding 10% of total 30-day periods in any calendar month shall trigger a root cause analysis per QA-AE-002.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| LUPA | Low Utilization Payment Adjustment — a payment reduction applied by CMS when the total number of visits in a 30-day payment period falls below the PDGM LUPA threshold for the assigned payment group. The agency receives a per-visit payment instead of the full PDGM case-mix adjusted payment. |
| LUPA Threshold | The minimum number of visits (by all disciplines combined) required in a 30-day period to receive the full PDGM payment. Thresholds vary by payment group and are published annually by CMS. Typically range from 2 to 6 visits. |
| At-Risk Episode | A 30-day period where projected or actual visit count is at or below the LUPA threshold with calendar days remaining in the period. |
| Visit Count | The total number of billable visits by all disciplines (SN, PT, OT, SLP, MSW, HHA) within a 30-day payment period. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 LUPA Threshold Monitoring

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain the current CMS LUPA threshold table within the billing system, updated annually upon CMS publication. | Revenue Cycle Director | Annually (by January 1) |
| 6.1.2 | For every open 30-day payment period, the billing system (or manual tracking) shall compare actual visit count against the LUPA threshold at the midpoint (day 15) and at day 25 of each 30-day period. | Revenue Cycle Director / Billing Coordinator | Day 15 and Day 25 |
| 6.1.3 | Any 30-day period where the visit count at day 15 is below 50% of the LUPA threshold shall be flagged as "LUPA Watch." | Revenue Cycle Director | Day 15 |
| 6.1.4 | Any 30-day period where the visit count at day 25 is at or below the LUPA threshold shall be flagged as "LUPA Alert." | Revenue Cycle Director | Day 25 |
| 6.1.5 | LUPA Watch and LUPA Alert flags shall be communicated to the Director of Nursing within 24 hours of identification. | Revenue Cycle Director | Within 24 hours |

### 6.2 Clinical Review of At-Risk Episodes

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Upon receipt of a LUPA Watch or Alert, the Director of Nursing shall review the episode within 48 hours. The review shall determine: (a) total visits ordered on the Plan of Care for this 30-day period; (b) total visits actually delivered; (c) reason for any shortfall (missed visits, cancellations, scheduling gaps, patient refusal, hospitalization); (d) whether clinical need supports the ordered frequency; (e) whether any visits can be legitimately rescheduled within the remaining days. | Director of Nursing | Within 48 hours |
| 6.2.2 | If visits were missed due to scheduling error, staff availability, or patient cancellation (not patient refusal or clinical reason): (a) reschedule missed visits within the remaining days of the 30-day period if clinically appropriate and consistent with the Plan of Care; (b) document the rescheduling and the clinical rationale per CL-SD-024 and CL-SD-025. | Director of Nursing / Scheduling Coordinator | Immediately |
| 6.2.3 | If the visit shortfall is due to the physician ordering fewer visits than the LUPA threshold and clinical need supports additional visits: the DON may contact the physician to discuss whether the visit frequency should be adjusted. Any change must be physician-ordered and documented. | Director of Nursing | Within 48 hours |
| 6.2.4 | If the visit count is below LUPA threshold due to legitimate clinical reasons (patient improved rapidly, patient refused visits, patient hospitalized during the period): document the clinical rationale, accept the LUPA payment, and close the review. No visits shall be added. | Director of Nursing | Document immediately |
| 6.2.5 | Document the LUPA review decision on the LUPA Review Worksheet (Appendix A). | Director of Nursing | Same day as decision |

### 6.3 Monthly LUPA Rate Monitoring

| Step | Action | Responsible Party | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | The Revenue Cycle Director shall calculate the monthly LUPA rate: (LUPA 30-day periods ÷ total 30-day periods billed) × 100. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly — by 15th of following month |
| 6.3.2 | Report LUPA rate to CFO monthly and include in the revenue cycle performance dashboard per FN-FP-003. | Revenue Cycle Director | Monthly |
| 6.3.3 | Report LUPA rate to QAPI Committee quarterly per QA-PI-006. | QAPI Coordinator | Quarterly |
| 6.3.4 | Report LUPA rate to Governing Body quarterly in the financial report per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5. | CFO | Quarterly |
| 6.3.5 | If LUPA rate exceeds 10% for any calendar month, initiate root cause analysis per QA-AE-002 within 30 calendar days. | Revenue Cycle Director + Compliance Officer | Within 30 calendar days |

### 6.4 Escalation Matrix

| Condition | Escalation Path | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Episode at LUPA Watch (day 15) | DON review within 48 hours | Day 15 |
| Episode at LUPA Alert (day 25) | DON urgent review and action | Day 25 — immediate |
| Monthly LUPA rate >10% | CFO and Compliance Officer; RCA per QA-AE-002 | Within 30 calendar days |
| Monthly LUPA rate >15% | Administrator and Governing Body notified | Within 5 business days |
| Pattern of visits added without clinical basis | Compliance Officer investigation per CO-CP-007 | Within 24 hours |
| Scheduling barriers causing chronic LUPA | Operations Director; staffing and scheduling review | Within 14 calendar days |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Retention |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA threshold table | Current CMS LUPA thresholds | Revenue Cycle Director | Billing system | Current year + 7 years |
| LUPA review | LUPA Review Worksheet (Appendix A) | Director of Nursing | Clinical/billing file | 7 years |
| Monthly LUPA rate | Revenue cycle dashboard per FN-FP-003 | Revenue Cycle Director | Financial records | 7 years |
| RCA (if triggered) | RCA report per QA-AE-002 | Compliance Officer | QAPI/compliance file | 7 years |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix B | Revenue Cycle Director | Policy acknowledgment file | 7 years |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA rate | Monthly calculation | ≤ 10% |
| LUPA Watch/Alert reviewed by DON within 48 hours | Appendix A timestamp | 100% |
| Zero visits added solely for LUPA avoidance | Compliance audit/investigation | Zero findings |
| Monthly LUPA reporting completed | Dashboard report | 100% |
| RCA triggered for LUPA rate >10__ | RCA documentation | 100% |

## 9. References
42 CFR § 484.205, 42 CFR § 484.60, CMS PDGM Final Rules, CMS IOM Pub. 100-04 Ch. 10.
Cross-referenced: FN-BC-001, FN-BC-007, FN-CM-001, FN-FP-003, CL-SD-024, CL-SD-025, OP-SL-001, QA-PI-002, QA-PI-006, QA-AE-002, CO-CP-007, CO-FA-003, GV-GB-001, EN-TG-001.

# FN-FP-001 — Payer Contract Management
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | FN-FP-001 |
| Title | Payer Contract Management |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | FP — Financial Planning & Performance |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Policy Owner/Steward | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |
| Regulatory Alignment | 42 CFR § 484.100, 42 CFR Part 422 (Medicare Advantage), State insurance regulations, ERISA (for self-funded plans) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes standards for negotiating, executing, monitoring, and managing contracts with Medicare Advantage plans, Medi-Cal managed care plans, and commercial insurance payers for home health services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Payer contracts define reimbursement rates, authorization requirements, billing procedures, and performance expectations that directly affect agency revenue and operations. Effective contract management ensures the agency: (a) maintains financially viable reimbursement rates; (b) complies with all contractual obligations; (c) tracks and enforces payer payment obligations; (d) evaluates contract performance annually; (e) makes informed decisions about contract renewals, renegotiations, and terminations.
## 3. Scope
CFO / Revenue Cycle Director, Administrator, Compliance Officer, Director of Nursing (clinical requirements), billing coordinators, legal counsel (as needed).
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 All payer contracts shall be reviewed and approved by the CFO before execution. Contracts with annualized revenue impact exceeding $50,000 shall also be approved by the Administrator. Contracts exceeding $250,000 shall be reported to the Governing Body.
4.2 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a centralized Payer Contract Registry (Appendix A) documenting all active contracts with key terms, rates, expiration dates, and renewal deadlines.
4.3 Each payer contract shall be reviewed for financial performance annually, including: reimbursement adequacy (comparison to Medicare FFS rates and cost of care delivery), payment timeliness, denial rates, and administrative burden.
4.4 Contract renewal decisions shall be based on documented financial analysis. No contract shall be auto-renewed without annual performance review.
4.5 The agency shall not accept reimbursement rates below the cost of service delivery without documented business justification approved by the CFO and Administrator.
4.6 All contracts shall be reviewed by the Compliance Officer for regulatory compliance and by legal counsel for contracts exceeding $100,000 or containing unusual terms.
## 5–11. Procedures, Documentation, Compliance, References, Training, Version Control
(Full procedures covering: contract negotiation, execution, registry maintenance, annual performance review, renewal/termination decisions, rate analysis methodology, escalation matrix. Documentation: Appendix A — Contract Registry; Appendix B — Annual Contract Performance Review; Appendix C — Contract Negotiation Checklist; Appendix D — Policy Acknowledgment. Compliance monitoring: 100% contracts in registry, annual reviews completed, no auto-renewals without review. References: 42 CFR § 484.100, state insurance law, cross-referenced FN-BC-007, FN-FP-003, GV-EA-001, CO-HP-005, GV-GB-001, EN-TG-001. Training and version control per standard format.)

## 2–4. Purpose, Scope, Policy Statements
Establishes standards for accurate charge capture for all services and maintenance of the agency fee schedule. Ensures: (a) all billable services are captured with correct charge codes, amounts, and dates; (b) the agency fee schedule is reviewed and updated annually; (c) charges are reconciled against services delivered; (d) charge capture errors are identified and corrected. Applies to all billing coordinators, Revenue Cycle Director, CFO, clinical scheduling coordinators.
## 5–11. Full Policy Content
(Procedures covering: charge capture workflow from visit delivery through billing; fee schedule annual update process aligned with CMS rate changes; charge reconciliation against visit logs; charge correction process; charge capture audit quarterly. Appendices: A — Fee Schedule; B — Charge Capture Reconciliation Log; C — Policy Acknowledgment. Compliance: 100% services captured, charge accuracy ≥99%, fee schedule updated annually by January 1. References: 42 CFR § 484.205, FN-BC-001, FN-CM-001, FN-FP-003, OP-SL-001, CO-HP-007, EN-TG-001.)
# FN-FP-003 — Revenue Cycle Performance Monitoring
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |

| Title | Revenue Cycle Performance Monitoring |
| Domain | FN — Finance & Revenue Cycle |
| Subdomain | FP — Financial Planning & Performance |

| Status | ACTIVE |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |

| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |

## 2. Purpose
Establishes a comprehensive revenue cycle performance monitoring program with defined KPIs, monitoring frequency, dashboard reporting, and escalation thresholds. Revenue cycle performance is a critical indicator of agency financial health and billing compliance.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 The Revenue Cycle Director shall maintain a Revenue Cycle Performance Dashboard (Appendix A) tracking the following KPIs monthly:

| KPI | Target | Escalation Threshold |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Days in A/R (average) | ≤ 45 days | > 60 days |
| Clean Claim Rate | ≥ 95% | < 90% |
| Denial Rate | ≤ 5% | > 7% |
| LUPA Rate | ≤ 10% | > 15% |
| NOA Timeliness (≤5 days) | 100% | < 95% |
| Claims Submission Timeliness (≤14 days from period end) | ≥ 95% | < 90% |
| Payment Variance Rate | < 3% | > 5% |
| Cash Collection Rate | ≥ 95% of expected | < 90% |
| Timely Filing Violations | Zero | Any |
| Overpayments Identified/Returned in 60 Days | 100% | Any violation |

4.2 Dashboard reviewed by CFO monthly, presented to Administrator monthly, reported to Governing Body quarterly.
4.3 Any KPI exceeding escalation threshold for 2 consecutive months triggers formal RCA per QA-AE-002.
## 6. Procedures
Monthly dashboard production by 20th of following month. CFO review within 5 business days. Quarterly integration with QAPI per QA-PI-002. Governing Body reporting per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.

## 2–11. Full Policy Content
Establishes criteria for classifying and managing bad debt write-offs and charity care at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Ensures: no patient denied medically necessary services due to inability to pay; financial hardship screening before collection referral; charity care eligibility based on FPL guidelines; bad debt write-off only after exhausting collection per FN-BC-003 §6.6; all write-offs require CFO authorization; annual reporting of bad debt and charity care to Governing Body.
Appendices: A — Charity Care Application; B — Bad Debt Write-Off Authorization; C — Policy Acknowledgment.

## 2. Purpose
Mandates annual budget development, Governing Body approval, and ongoing variance monitoring per 42 CFR § 484.105 (Governing Body financial oversight) and GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 The CFO shall prepare an annual operating budget for the upcoming fiscal year no later than 60 calendar days before fiscal year start.
4.2 The budget shall include: projected revenue by payer, projected expenses by category, capital expenditure plan, staffing plan with costs, and projected net operating margin.
4.3 The Governing Body shall review and approve the budget no later than 30 calendar days before fiscal year start per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.1.
4.4 Monthly variance monitoring: the CFO shall compare actual results to budget monthly and report variances to the Administrator. Variance >10% from budget in any major category triggers corrective action.
4.5 Quarterly budget-to-actual reports presented to Governing Body per GV-GB-001 §6.2.5.2.
Appendices: A — Annual Budget Template; B — Monthly Variance Report; C — Policy Acknowledgment.
